# Decisions log

Format: date · decision · reason · alternatives considered.

- **2026-09-28 · Domain = university-level CS (computer networking).** Reason: the project is meant to use university-level content. Alternatives: Beetle/SciEntsBank (school science; they have gold incomplete-vs-contradictory labels) — kept only as an optional sanity check.
- **2026-09-28 · Primary dataset = SAF Communication Networks (English).** Reason: university course; explanatory answers (~82 tokens on average); human-written feedback on what is wrong or missing; CC BY 4.0; unseen-question split. Limitations: no incomplete/contradictory label (derive silver labels, M4); no student IDs.
- **2026-09-28 · Expert source = Peterson & Davie, *Computer Networks: A Systems Approach* 6e.** Reason: open (CC BY 4.0), structured reStructuredText source, covers most SAF topics. Check coverage per question in M1 (DQDB, queueing, frame bursting may be thin).
- **2026-09-28 · Second domain (stretch) = Mohler data structures + *Open Data Structures*.** Reason: allows comparison with MitiGaTe (RMSE 0.762); strong published misconception research for data structures and hashing.
- **2026-09-28 · LLM provider = OpenAI (Responses API + Structured Outputs).** Strong tier `gpt-6-astra`, bulk tier `gpt-6-luna`; both configurable. Reason: user choice. All calls are cached and version-logged.
- **2026-09-28 · Graph store = NetworkX + JSONL during the MVP; Neo4j export optional.** Reason: simplicity and reproducibility; Neo4j only for visualisation/queries later.
- **2026-09-28 · Relation registry v0 = 14 relations** (see `configs/relations_v0.yaml`) — awaiting human approval in M2.
- **2026-09-28 · Evaluation priority:** the human-verified label subset (M4) is the primary test set; silver labels are secondary. Reason: the silver labeller is an LLM, so evaluating only against it would be circular.
- **2026-09-28 · M0: verified `openai` SDK (installed version 3.19.2) against CLAUDE.md's description of the Responses API — no deviations found.** `Responses.parse(model=, input=, text_format=, reasoning=, ...)` exists as described; `response.output_parsed` is a computed property on `ParsedResponse`; `response.usage.input_tokens` / `.output_tokens` exist on `ResponseUsage`. `reasoning` is a TypedDict with an `effort` field whose literal values include `none, minimal, low, medium, high, xhigh, max` — the installed SDK's type does allow `effort="none"` (unlike the informal claim that "none" is unsupported generally); CLAUDE.md's rule is scoped to the specific model `gpt-6-astra` not accepting it, so `LLMClient` always passes `reasoning={"effort": ...}` and only coerces `none → low` when the target model is `gpt-6-astra`. No `configs/default.yaml` changes were needed.
- **2026-09-28 · `data/gold/` and `data/interim/` are committed (not gitignored); only `data/raw/`, `data/processed/`, `data/cache/`, `data/logs/` and `.env` are ignored**, per BUILD_PLAN M0 task 8's explicit list. `.gitkeep` placeholders added so the empty `data/gold/` and `data/interim/suggestions/` directories exist in a fresh clone.
- **2026-09-28 · M1: textbook parser is heading-regex, not docutils** (BUILD_PLAN said "try docutils first"). Reason: the book uses many Sphinx-only directives (`figure`, `numref`, `table`, custom roles); registering pass-throughs for all of them in docutils was more machinery than a direct text extractor for this use case. Chosen granularity: **one Section per toctree-leaf `.rst` file**, not per in-file heading depth — the book's own nesting (root `index.rst` → chapter file → chapter's own toctree of section files) already gives exactly the "lowest-level heading" granularity BUILD_PLAN asks for (e.g. `congestion/tcpcc.rst` → section_id "6.3"). Deeper in-file subheadings (e.g. "6.3.1 ...") are kept as inline paragraph text, not split into separate Sections. Verified on the real 6th-edition source: 64 sections, all 9 numbered chapters + 3 front-matter files, 0 sections under 20 words, `order_index` strictly increasing, 0 residual RST markup found in a full-book scan (one "false positive" was an ellipsis `...` in ordinary prose).
- **2026-09-28 · M1 finding: SAF `score` is not a global 0–1 fraction** — it is an absolute point value whose maximum varies per question (1.0 to 3.5 across the 26 seen questions), confirmed by `verification_feedback == "Correct"` always landing at that question's own max. M4 must normalise `score / per_question_max_score` before using it as a proposition-weight hint; using raw `score` directly would be wrong. Logged in `reports/m1_eda_saf.md` §2.
- **2026-09-28 · M1 finding: 26 "seen" questions (train/validation/test_unseen_answers, identical set) + 5 disjoint "unseen" questions (test_unseen_questions) = 31 distinct question texts total.** No question overlap between seen and unseen splits (confirms the unseen-questions split is genuinely novel-topic, not just reworded). Two of the 26 seen questions are cosmetic rewordings of the same TCP-congestion-control question (differ only in a trailing "total." vs "."), which likely explains the paper's count of 22 vs the dataset card's 26.
- **2026-09-28 · 👤 Human-approved pilot questions (5, recorded in `configs/default.yaml`):** `q_ad9ae0e3` (piggybacking -> 2.5), `q_57a5b057` (transparent bridges -> 3.2 §3.2.1), `q_cc7ede94` (IPv6 extension headers -> 4.2), `q_58c81db9` (Reverse Path Forwarding/Broadcast -> 4.3), `q_582d0a1c` (TCP congestion control -> 6.3+6.4). **Selection basis: not a literature-grounded choice.** BUILD_PLAN's own candidate list was the starting point; each candidate was then checked directly against the cloned textbook (not just embeddings) for real coverage in this edition, and against train-split label spread (Correct/Partially correct/Incorrect) to favor questions with diagnostic variety. DQDB and frame bursting were dropped (not covered in this 6th edition, confirmed by direct search); CSMA/CD collision domain was dropped despite full coverage because 55/57 train answers are already "Correct" (little incomplete/contradictory signal). For 3 of the 5 approved questions (piggybacking, bridges, RPF/RPB), the section that actually covers the topic falls **outside the embedding top-5 entirely** — the automated `coverage_guess` column in `data/interim/suggestions/question_section_map.csv` is wrong for those rows (says none/partial); the human-reviewed, corrected mapping is `data/interim/suggestions/pilot_question_section_map.csv` (`verified_section_id`/`verified_coverage`/`verification_note` columns). Per CLAUDE.md rule 2, this file was **not** written to `data/gold/` — the human still needs to review and copy it there themselves.
- **2026-09-28 · M1 coverage-guess LLM pricing is a placeholder** (`configs/default.yaml`: `assumed_usd_per_1k_*_tokens`). `gpt-6-astra`/`gpt-6-luna` have no published per-token price found; the `--dry-run` cost estimate uses an assumed rate pending confirmation, and is order-of-magnitude only.
- **2026-09-28 · Fixed a bug in the coverage-guess prompt: sections were truncated to 800 chars, which silently cut off the relevant paragraph in every section longer than ~150 words** (median section is 2788 words). First run returned "none"/"partial" for every one of the 31 questions, including ones with directly-confirmed full textbook coverage (e.g. piggybacking, IPv6 extension headers). Re-ran with full, untruncated section text for the top-3 candidates (actual cost: ~515K input / ~4K output tokens across both runs, ~$1.2 combined under placeholder pricing — still under the $5/command threshold, so re-run without a second approval round-trip; flagged to the user afterward since the estimate had grown ~15x from the first quote).
- **2026-09-28 · M2 schema/registry implementation notes.** (1) `cumap gold validate` dispatches by path convention under `data/gold/` (`expert_pilot/*.yaml` -> `list[ExpertEdge]`, `student_pilot/*.yaml` -> `list[StudentEdge]`, `propositions.jsonl` -> `Proposition` per line, `verified_labels.jsonl` -> `SilverAnswerLabel` per line, `misconceptions*.yaml` -> `list[Misconception]`); anything else is **skipped, not failed**, since this is a human-owned directory that may hold files with no pydantic schema yet (e.g. `question_section_map.csv`). (2) `Evidence` requires exactly one of `section_id`/`answer_id` (a model validator) so quote verification always has an unambiguous source to check against. (3) `RelationRegistry.is_reversal` relies on `normalise()` already canonicalising symmetric-relation endpoint order, so a symmetric relation's "reversed" pair normalises to the identical edge and is correctly reported as *not* a reversal, without a separate special case. (4) `check_types`/evidence-substring checks only flag *known* concepts/sections as wrong — missing lookups (concept not yet in the KG, unknown section) are reported as their own distinct issue, never silently treated as a type violation.
- **2026-09-28 · Residual limitation: coverage-guess quality is capped by embedding top-3 retrieval, not just the truncation bug.** For "transparent bridges" and "piggybacking", the section that actually covers the topic (3.2 Switched Ethernet §3.2.1 "Learning Bridges"; 2.5 Reliable Transmission) ranked outside the embedding top-3 (ranks 4-5), so the LLM correctly judged "none"/"partial" *given what it was shown*, disagreeing with direct manual verification against the book text. This is the project's own stated principle in action ("embeddings only propose candidates; the verdict comes from checked evidence") — treat the automated `coverage` column as a first pass, not a substitute for checking the actual top-5 list (or the book) before ruling a question out. Same caution applies going into M5's concept/relation retrieval.
- **2026-09-28 · CR-001 applied (registry v1, edge qualifiers, ChainLink layer, version-aware validator, gold migration tool, v2 suggest prompts, LLM tiers pending).** Source: `docs/change-requests/CR-001-relations-v1.md`, rationale in `docs/research/relation-mapping-research.md`. Registry v0 (14 relations) kept loadable; v1 (16 relations, 6 families + pedagogical) is now the config default. Superseded, not contradicted: the 2026-09-28 "LLM provider" entry's model pins (`gpt-6-astra` strong / `gpt-6-luna` bulk) will be revisited in CR-001 §8 (tiers/escalation), not yet done as of this entry. `data/gold/expert_pilot/q_*.yaml` were drafted for real with the new v2 prompt (`cumap gold suggest-expert --all`) to unblock §7.3's sampler — 0 rejections across all 5 pilot questions, 12 chain links proposed.
- **2026-09-28 · CR-001 §7.3 relation-agreement sampler: widened from single-sentence to paragraph-level co-occurrence, and strips a concept name's trailing parenthetical gloss before matching.** Reason: CR-001's text says "sentences", but on the real 5 pilot-question sections this found only 5 candidate items total (nowhere near the ~48 needed for 6 families × 8) — mostly because LLM-drafted concept names like "congestion avoidance (additive increase)" never appear verbatim in the source text, and genuine relations are often stated across multiple sentences within one paragraph rather than within a single sentence. Paragraph-level + parenthetical-stripping recovered 30 candidates (see PROGRESS.md for the shortfall this still leaves — flagged to the human as a STOP 2 decision point, not silently resolved).
- **2026-09-28 · CR-001 §8: verified `gpt-6-sol` works (low and medium reasoning effort) via a real API call before making it the strong-tier default** — same diligence as M0's `gpt-6-astra` check. `configs/default.yaml` now has `llm.tiers.{strong,bulk,ceiling}` (replacing the flat `model_strong`/`model_bulk`/`reasoning_effort` fields) plus `llm.escalation`; `.env.example` and the user's own `.env` updated to match (`OPENAI_MODEL_CEILING` added; `OPENAI_MODEL_STRONG` now defaults to `gpt-6-sol`). `LLMClient` gained a generic escalation path: a bulk-tier call retries once on the strong tier on a schema-validation failure, a sub-threshold `confidence` field, or a caller-supplied `escalate_check` predicate (for post-hoc checks like evidence verification, which happen outside the client) — never more than one retry, logged with `escalated`/`escalation_reason`.
- **2026-09-28 · 👤 Scope decision: focus only on the expert knowledge graph until further notice. Student-side work is deferred, not cancelled.** Deferred: **M4** (silver labels from SAF feedback), **M6** (expected subgraphs + student-answer extraction), **M7** (alignment/diagnosis/evaluation), **M8** (misconceptions/feedback/second domain), **M3's student-annotation half** (`data/gold/student_pilot/*`, the relation-agreement test's student-graph parts — M3's *expert*-annotation half, `data/gold/expert_pilot/*`, stays in scope), **CR-003 B3** (SemEval-2013 diagnosis, which is student-answer diagnosis by definition) **and CR-003's other student-side parts** (§7.2 student pilot check-only mode, §8 items 3/4 — student answers and the M4 check sheet), and **CR-004 §8–§10** (per-question mini-maps, which need M6 expected subgraphs; misconception perturbations, which need M7 match types on student data; and the new M7 diagnostic outputs section itself). Reason: not logged by the owner beyond "focusing only on the expert knowledge graph" — recorded here as the scope boundary so later milestones aren't picked back up by accident. Marked "deferred" (not removed) in `BUILD_PLAN.md` and `PROGRESS.md`.
- **2026-09-29 · CR-005/CR-003 §3: `iir_face` loader — source, commit, licence, and the real B1 dev split (recorded before any API call).** Source: `github.com/PAWSLabUniversityOfPittsburgh/Concept-Extraction`, commit `9f03208fd995fc4e92c14fa1d371c185629ead56` (2021-11-22), cited via Wang et al. 2021 (*Technology, Knowledge and Learning*) and Chau et al. 2020 (IJAIED, the FACE paper). **No LICENSE file in the repo** — used for local, non-redistributed academic research only (comparing this project's own extraction against the paper's published numbers), matching CR-003 §3's framing; the underlying text/labels are never committed (`data/raw/` and the new `data/interim/external/` are both gitignored — see `.gitignore` and `data/raw/external/iir_face/SOURCE.md`). **B1 dev chapters = chapters 1–3, and this isn't just a reasonable default: it's forced.** The repo's `book_section_samples/iir.sections.txt` only contains section *text* for chapters 1–3 (13 sections: `iir_1`+`iir_1_1..1_4`, `iir_2_1..2_4`, `iir_3_1..3_4`); the other 73 sections (chapters 4–16) have gold concept-label CSVs but no accompanying text in this repo, so running B1 test (or any of chapters 4–16) would need sourcing text separately — out of scope per CR-005 §6 ("skip the IIR test chapters for now"). Gold concept labels are a 3-annotator majority vote (≥2 of 3 say "yes") per candidate, read from `IIR-dataset/annotation/*.csv`. **Real counts from `cumap external load iir_face` (replaces the earlier ~16-section, ~49,500-word estimate):** 13 sections across 3 chapters, **17,987 words total** — ch1 "Boolean retrieval" (5 sections, 4,683 words), ch2 "The term vocabulary and postings lists" (4 sections, 8,065 words), ch3 "Dictionaries and tolerant retrieval" (4 sections, 5,239 words). 603 candidate concepts, 485 gold (majority vote ≥2/3 annotators) — a much richer concept density than P&D's own sections, and the actual corpus is smaller than the earlier estimate assumed, so the LLM-call cost estimate needs redoing (see CR-005's next entry).
- **2026-09-28 · CR-005 (expert-KG demo slice) started at STOP 1: plan + dry-run cost estimate, no code written yet.** Slice definition: P&D chapters 1–3 (real: 24 sections, 74,271 words — Foundation/Direct Links/Internetworking, covering the encoding, piggybacking, CSMA/CD and bridges pilot topics) + FACE/IIR B1 dev split (3 chapters, ~16 sections estimated from the FACE paper's 86-sections/16-chapters density — **unconfirmed**, since the `iir_face` loader hasn't been built/run yet). Cost estimate (~$5.56 under placeholder pricing, vs CR-005's own $15–30 reference estimate at unconfirmed "list prices" — both are estimates, not measurements; flagged the gap rather than picking one) built from stated per-section call-count assumptions (concept extraction incl. one gleaning pass, canonicalisation, relation Stage B family-first choice, qualifier pass), not yet from an actual dry-run of real code (none of the extraction pipeline exists yet — that's what steps after STOP 1 build).
- **2026-09-28 · CR-001 §7.3 relation-agreement sampler is deferred, not fixed further.** The 30-vs-≥48-item shortfall (logged earlier) is superseded by CR-003 §6 ("Relation-registry agreement without a second human"), which replaces the sampler with cross-model agreement (150 P&D sentences, strong vs ceiling tier) plus a 60-item human-blind anchor — a fundamentally different method, not a bigger version of the same sampler. No further work on `cumap gold sample-relation-items`/`cumap eval relation-agreement` until CR-003 §6 is reached; the 30 items already sampled and the scorer code stay as-is (tested, unused for now).
- **2026-09-28 · CR-001 §10 acceptance checks — run and recorded honestly (5 of 7 clean, 2 flagged, not silently passed).** ✅ `uv run pytest`: 150/150 pass with `OPENAI_API_KEY` unset and no network. ✅ `ruff check .` clean. ✅ `cumap gold validate` passes on both v0 and v1 fixtures, and cleanly on the real (still-empty) `data/gold/` under default and `--registry 1` rules — the "passes on `data/gold/` after the human migrates" half of this check can't be verified yet since there's nothing to migrate. ✅ `reports/migration_v1.md` exists (real run, currently empty since `data/gold/` is empty); the read-only-to-`data/gold/` proof is a real test (chmod'd directory). ⚠️ Dry-run estimates now show tier name + model (`suggest-expert`/`suggest-student`/`textbook coverage --dry-run`); batch pricing display is N/A since no `--batch` flag exists anywhere (Batch API itself deferred, see below) — partially met by design, not an oversight. ✅ Docs updated per §9. ❌ **Relation-agreement sampler: real run produced 30 items, not the ≥48 (6×8) target** — logged earlier, still an open decision for the human (accept as-is / widen beyond pilot sections / lower the per-family minimum). This is the one check CR-001 explicitly required that the real data doesn't support yet; not silently declared "done."
- **2026-09-29 · CR-005 §2: M5 subset built as real M5 code (`src/cumap/expert_kg/`), all mock-backend tested, no API calls yet.** New modules: `stats.py` (candidate-term signals, no LLM), `concepts.py` (per-section extraction + one gleaning pass, roles defined/used/mentioned, evidence-verified), `canonicalize.py` (top-5 similar existing concepts by name+definition embedding; below-threshold = new concept with no LLM call, above-threshold = LLM same/broader/narrower/different), `relations.py` (Stage A candidate pairs by same-paragraph co-occurrence, no LLM, deduped once per section; Stage B family choice then relation choice as **two separate calls**, not one coarse-to-fine call, so each stage's own accuracy/cost is measurable — followed by a separate qualifier-pass call; family=no_relation, relation=other, or a failed evidence/surface_phrase check each stop the pipeline early with a `reason`, never entering the graph), `prerequisites.py` (defined→used-later candidates and forward-references, rule-based over each concept's own mention history plus book-order section IDs), `checks.py` (domain/range + cycles over every transitive relation, reusing `RelationRegistry.check_types`/`find_cycles`), `snapshots.py` (per-chapter JSONL+manifest writer/loader; growth metrics computed at read time from a snapshot sequence, not stored per snapshot — a chapter's `nodes`/`edges`/`merges` are a *delta*, so cumulative totals are a running union across chapters). Corresponding new prompts: `prompts/{concept_extraction,concept_extraction_gleaning,canonicalize,relation_family,relation_choice,relation_qualifiers}/v1.md`. 39 new tests across 6 test files, all passing (mock backend, no network/key). **Model tier correction caught before any real call:** `canonicalize.py` and `relations.py` were first written on the `bulk` tier by analogy with `concepts.py`; corrected to `strong` per CR-005 §1 ("bulk tier for concepts... strong tier for relations") and CLAUDE.md's tier table (`strong` = "KG construction... judging" — canonicalisation's same/broader/narrower/different call and every relation-extraction call are both judgement calls, not per-item bulk labelling). `pipeline.py` (chapter-by-chapter orchestration) is intentionally not built yet — Step 4's calibration run (1 IIR section + 1 P&D section) doesn't need a generalised multi-chapter orchestrator, and building one before a real run has validated the pieces individually would be speculative.
- **2026-09-29 · CR-005 §2 step 3: cost estimate redone with tier-aware pricing and reasoning tokens counted as output; confirmed against OpenAI's current pricing page, no discrepancy.** Added `usd_per_1m_input_tokens`/`usd_per_1m_output_tokens` to `TierConfig` (`configs/default.yaml` `llm.tiers.*`) alongside the older flat `assumed_usd_per_1k_*` placeholder (left as-is — still used by the unrelated textbook-coverage dry-run, out of scope here). Web search confirmed OpenAI's published GPT-6 Sol/Luna pricing (2026-09) is **exactly** $2/$10 per 1M in/out (Sol) and $0.10/$0.50 per 1M in/out (Luna) — matching the owner-supplied figures with no discrepancy to flag. Bonus finding not yet used: cached input reads get a 90% discount ($0.20 Sol / $0.01 Luna) — worth exploiting later since `LLMClient` already caches by input hash, but the estimator below doesn't account for it (would only lower the number, so ignoring it is conservative, not risky). Astra (ceiling) has no confirmed price anywhere; left `None` in config, opt-in only, unused by this slice. `LLMClient._call_openai` now also captures `usage.output_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` (OpenAI already bills these as part of `output_tokens`; captured separately just for cost-report transparency, not double-counted). New `src/cumap/expert_kg/cost_estimate.py` (`estimate_slice_cost`, 4 tests) computes cost from named `CallAssumptions` (nothing hidden) — the pre-calibration estimate for IIR dev (13 sections) + P&D ch.1–3 (24 sections) is **≈$11.04** (IIR ≈$3.88, P&D ≈$7.16), assuming **20 candidate pairs/section** (well under Step 5's ~60/section stop line) and, per pair, a family call always, a relation call for ~70% of pairs, a qualifier call for ~55%. Concept extraction (bulk) is cheap (~$0.08 total); canonicalisation and relations (strong tier) dominate (~$10.9 combined), consistent with CR-005 §8's own estimate that relations are "the main cost." All of this is still an assumption-based estimate, not a measurement — Step 4's calibration run (1 real IIR section + 1 real P&D section) replaces the `CallAssumptions` defaults with measured numbers before the full IIR-dev run.
- **2026-09-29 · CR-005 §2 step 4: calibration run (real API) stopped early — Step 5's stop condition triggered, no full IIR-dev run started.** Ran the real pipeline end to end on one representative IIR dev section (`iir_2_1`, "The term vocabulary and postings lists", 1,356 words — close to the IIR-dev average of 1,384 words/section) via a one-off driver script (not committed; calls only the already-committed `src/cumap/expert_kg/*` functions). **Stopped mid-run, before reaching the second (P&D) calibration section**, once `relation_family` calls for this single section reached 60 and were still climbing — CR-005 §2 step 5's own line ("candidate pairs run above ~60 per section, stop and show me first") was crossed by Stage A's real (deterministic, no-LLM) pairing count, not a cost overrun per se. Real numbers, not guesses: 16 concepts from the main extraction pass + 6 new ones from gleaning (vs. the 30/section assumed in step 3 — concept counts were *lower* than assumed); **≥60 candidate pairs from those ~22 concepts** (a lower bound — Stage A's own pairing is a single deterministic pass with no LLM cost, but the run was killed partway through Stage B/C's per-pair loop, so the *true* total for even this one section is unknown and likely higher). Only ~23% of concepts (5/22) actually triggered a real canonicalisation LLM call (vs. the 40% assumed — cheaper than expected, using real `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` embeddings, not the unit tests' toy bag-of-words). Measured avg tokens/call (input / total-output-including-reasoning): `concept_extraction` 2873/964, `concept_extraction_gleaning` 2825/664, `canonicalize` 452/145, `relation_family` 1947/172, `relation_choice` 2066/310, `relation_qualifiers` 2058/307. **Actual spend so far: $0.83** (one section, concepts+canonicalisation+partial relations only). **Re-estimating the full slice (IIR dev 13 sections + P&D ch.1–3 24 sections) with these measured per-call numbers, using 60 candidate pairs/section as a conservative lower bound, gives ≈$30.80 total** (IIR ≈$10.82, P&D ≈$19.98) — relations (strong tier) are ~97% of this, concept extraction (bulk) is negligible (~$0.02 total), consistent with CR-005 §8's own framing. This is at/over both of Step 5's stop conditions (>$30 total, >~60 pairs/section) using a number that likely *understates* the true pairs count, and P&D's sections average ~3x longer than the calibration IIR section, so pairs/section there could run higher still. **Per CR-005 §2 step 5, stopping here rather than starting the full IIR-dev run** — this needs the owner's decision (raise the budget; tighten Stage A's co-occurrence rule, e.g. require the SAME sentence rather than paragraph, or cap pairs/section; drop the qualifier pass's separateness and fold it into relation_choice; or accept the higher cost as-is). `max_usd_per_command` left at $5 (not raised to $15) since CR-005 §2 step 5 ties that change to actually being cleared to proceed, which hasn't happened.
- **2026-09-29 · 👤 CR-005 §2 step 5: owner decision — reduce P&D from ch.1–3 to ch.2–3, keep IIR dev at all 3 chapters; `max_usd_per_command` raised from $5 to $15.** Given the step-4 finding (candidate pairs/section well above the step-3 assumption, full-slice re-estimate ≈$30.80 at a conservative floor), three options were put to the owner: tighten Stage A pairing, reduce scope, or accept the cost as-is. Chose scope reduction, keeping IIR dev's B1 split intact (CR-003 §4) rather than narrowing the FACE-benchmark comparison. **P&D ch1 "Foundation" is dropped** — verified by direct text search that none of the 4 named pilot-topic keywords (encoding, piggyback, csma/carrier sense, bridge/spanning tree) occur anywhere in its 7 sections/16,983 words; all four are confirmed present in ch2 "Direct Links" (encoding §2.2, piggybacking §2.5, CSMA/CD §2.6) and ch3 "Internetworking" (bridges/spanning tree §3.2, §3.5) — so this cut loses no pilot-topic coverage. New `configs/demo_slice.yaml` records the finalised slice (P&D chapters [2,3], IIR dev chapters [1,2,3]). Re-estimated total with this scope, same measured per-call numbers and the same conservative 60-pairs/section floor as step 4: **P&D ch2–3 ≈$14.17 (17 sections, down from $20.00/24 sections) + IIR dev (unchanged) ≈$10.83 = ≈$24.99 combined**, under the (now $15) `max_usd_per_command` per-command cap and comfortably under the original $30 slice-level gate. **Residual risk, disclosed to the owner and accepted implicitly by not choosing the "tighten Stage A pairing" option:** 60 pairs/section is a *floor*, not a measured ceiling — the calibration run was stopped mid-loop while still climbing, and P&D's sections average ~3x longer than the IIR section calibrated against, so real P&D pairs/section could run higher. Before the real IIR-dev run, `expert_kg/pipeline.py` (next entry) adds live per-command spend tracking against `max_usd_per_command`, since no code enforced the $5/$15 cap as a hard stop until now — the estimate alone was never a safety mechanism, only a prediction.
- **2026-09-29 · CR-005 §2 step 5: the real IIR-dev run was started, manually stopped at $14.03 (under the $15 cap), and produced no usable snapshot — found and need to fix a real design bug (candidate pairs aren't deduped across a chapter's sections) before trying again.** Ran `pipeline.run_slice` for real (run_id `iirdev_8715e199`) against all 13 IIR-dev sections. It never got past **chapter 1's 5th and final section** (`iir_1_4`): by the time it was manually killed, that one section alone had driven relation calls to 1,265 `relation_family` + 911 `relation_choice` + 539 `relation_qualifiers` (2,804 real calls total, $14.03 spent), with no sign of finishing. **Root cause, found by watching it live:** `relations.find_candidate_pairs` dedupes pairs only *within a single call* (i.e. within one section), not across a chapter — so every time two concepts that already co-occurred in an earlier section of the *same chapter* co-occur again in a later section, the pair is re-proposed and re-billed for a fresh family+relation+qualifier call, even though it was already classified. Because `extract_relations_for_section` is always given the *full* accumulated registry (`concept_registry.all()`, not just this section's new concepts — that's intentional, per CR-005 §2 task 4, "how cross-chapter edges arise"), and foundational IR terms (index, term, query, document, ...) recur across nearly every section of a chapter, this makes pair counts grow roughly with (concepts × how many sections already share them), not just with one section's own concept count — invisible in step 4's single-section calibration, which by construction couldn't exercise cross-section repetition. **Why the $15 cap didn't stop it automatically:** `run_slice` only checks `spend_for_run` against the cap *between chapters*, so a single chapter whose own cost exceeds the cap runs to completion (or, here, was manually killed first) before the check ever fires — a granularity gap, not a logic bug in the check itself. **Result: $14.03 of real spend produced zero committed snapshots** (chapter 1 never finished, so `write_snapshot` was never called) — a costly non-outcome, disclosed rather than glossed over. **Before any further real run:** (1) `find_candidate_pairs` (or its caller) needs a *run-level* (not per-call) seen-pairs set, keyed by the canonicalized concept-id pair, so a pair already classified earlier in the same chapter (or run) is skipped, not re-billed; (2) the budget check in `run_slice` should move to *per-section*, not per-chapter, so a single expensive section can't consume an entire chapter's (or the whole run's) budget unchecked. Neither fix is implemented yet — flagging both here rather than re-running against the same known-broken cost profile.
- **2026-09-29 · 👤 CR-005 §9: full amendment after the $14.03 IIR-dev overrun (previous entry) — scope split, client-level budget enforcement, sentence-level run-deduped candidate pairs, and a stage-based checkpointable pipeline.** Owner's response to the overrun: "go ahead with the fixes, but widen them." Six changes, all implemented and tested (mock backend, no real spend) before any further real run:
  1. **Scope split: relations only run on P&D.** IIR dev is concepts-only (extraction + FACE scoring) — FACE has no relation labels, so a relation stage there has nothing to validate against. `configs/demo_slice.yaml` gained `relations_enabled` per corpus (`pd: true`, `iir_face: false`), loaded via a new `config.load_demo_slice()` / `DemoSliceConfig` (not yet wired into `Settings` itself — a standalone loader, since the two config files serve different purposes: `default.yaml` is global, `demo_slice.yaml` is this one slice's own definition).
  2. **Budget enforcement moved into `LLMClient.parse` itself, checked before every call.** New `src/cumap/llm/cost.py` (`spend_from_log`) reads real spend for a `run_id` from the call log — moved here (not left in `expert_kg/cost_estimate.py`, which now just re-exports `spend_for_run = spend_from_log`) because `llm/` is the shared foundation and shouldn't depend on `expert_kg/`. `LLMClient` now seeds `self._spent_usd` from `spend_from_log` at construction (so `--resume` picks up prior real spend, not zero), estimates each call's worst case (estimated input tokens × tier price + `max_output_tokens` × tier price) before making it, and raises `BudgetExceededError` — a new exception — if spend-so-far-plus-worst-case would exceed the applicable cap. `TierConfig`/`LLMConfig` gained `max_usd_per_command` (existing) plus new `stage_budgets_usd` (per-stage caps, `configs/default.yaml` `llm.stage_budgets_usd`, all `null`/no-cap by default — a specific run passes a tighter override) and `max_output_tokens` (per-task worst-case ceiling, generous relative to Step 4's measured averages). `max_output_tokens` is also now passed to the *real* API call (`responses.parse(..., max_output_tokens=...)`), which is the direct fix for the runaway: a single call can no longer generate an unbounded amount of billed reasoning, which is exactly what happened to `iir_1_4`. Cache hits are exempted from the check (they cost nothing).
  3. **Candidate pairs: sentence-level (not paragraph), ranked, capped, and deduped for the life of the run (not per-section).** `relations.find_candidate_pairs` now takes a `window` (default 0 = same sentence only) and `max_pairs` (default 30/section, ranked by relational-cue-word presence then co-occurrence count then earliest position; pairs beyond the cap are returned separately as `overflow` and must never be classified). New `PairRegistry`, keyed by the pair's canonical (unordered) concept-id pair: `resolve()` marks a pair terminal — **`NO_RELATION` counts as resolved**, same as an accepted edge or any other rejection — and a later section that re-proposes an already-resolved pair calls `add_evidence()` instead of re-classifying, with zero further LLM calls. This is the direct fix for the root cause found live during the IIR-dev run: the same pair recurring across a chapter's sections was being silently re-billed every time. `classify_candidate_pair`'s evidence context changed from a paragraph to a single sentence, which changes prompt content enough to warrant new prompt versions (CLAUDE.md rule 8: never edit a version with real cached results) — `prompts/relation_{family,choice,qualifiers}/v2.md` created; v1 untouched.
  3b. **Cue words are registry-driven, not a second hardcoded relation list.** `_cue_words()` combines a small curated set of relation-indicating verbs/phrases with the registry's own `chain_link_types[*].cues` (already-curated linguistic connectives, ARCHITECTURE.md §4e) — used only to *rank* candidate pairs, never to decide a relation.
  4. **Pipeline rewritten stage-by-stage, not section-by-section-interleaved.** `expert_kg/pipeline.py`: (a) `run_concepts_stage` — all sections, bulk tier; (b) `run_canonicalize_stage` — book order, builds the growing `ConceptRegistry`; (c) `enumerate_pairs_stage` — **offline, no LLM call**, simulates the same run-level pair dedup a real relations stage would apply (a local `seen_pair_keys` set) so its reported call count is *exact*, not an over-count from re-proposed pairs — this is what STOP 1b's pair-count report will come from; (d) `run_relations_stage` — real LLM calls, only invoked explicitly (never automatically after (c)); (e) `build_snapshots_stage` — builds `ChapterSnapshot`s from the finished book-order state (concepts, edges, merges, prerequisites/forward-references — now correctly wired to `prerequisites.py`, which the old per-chapter pipeline also used but this one computes once at the end from complete state rather than incrementally per chapter).
  5. **Checkpointing + `--resume`, at section and stage granularity.** New `Checkpoint` dataclass (`save_checkpoint`/`load_checkpoint`, JSON at `<run_dir>/checkpoint.json`) serialises `ConceptRegistry` and `PairRegistry` state (via new `ConceptRegistry.restore()` / `PairRegistry.restore()` methods, added so pipeline.py doesn't reach into their private dicts) minus `RegisteredConcept.embedding` (not JSON-safe; recomputed lazily from `embed_fn` as before). A `BudgetExceededError` raised mid-stage is caught by that stage function, which saves the checkpoint (completed sections so far, not the interrupted one) and re-raises — the caller decides what to do next (stop, or fix the cap and resume). Because interrupted work isn't checkpointed below the section level, a resumed section restarts from its first sub-call, but the LLM cache means any of that section's calls that already succeeded are a cache hit on retry, not a re-bill.
  6. **Regression tests, all passing, mock backend, no network/key** — the four items §9 explicitly asked for: a pair repeated across 3 sections is classified exactly once (`test_expert_kg_relations.py`); the budget stops a run mid-run with a checkpoint saved (`test_llm_client.py`, `test_expert_kg_pipeline.py`); the pair cap holds and overflow is reported, not dropped (`test_expert_kg_relations.py`); `configs/demo_slice.yaml`'s IIR-dev corpus has `relations_enabled: False` (`test_expert_kg_pipeline.py`). 30 new/changed tests total across `llm/client.py`, `llm/cost.py`, `relations.py`, `pipeline.py`, `canonicalize.py`; 222/222 project-wide, ruff clean.

  **Not yet done, by design, per the execution order the owner specified:** the real IIR-dev concepts-only run (approved if ≤$2, its own STOP 2 with FACE P/R/F1 — the FACE scorer itself doesn't exist yet either); the real P&D concepts+canonicalize+offline-pair-enumeration run (STOP 1b: real pair counts per section before/after the cap, and the exact relation-call cost); and the relations stage itself, which does not run until the owner OKs STOP 1b's numbers. Hard cap for all of CR-005 from here: $15 total (distinct from the $15 *per-command* `max_usd_per_command` — tracked manually across commands until/unless a cross-run total tracker is built, which hasn't been asked for yet).

Lenient match (+lemma via spaCy, +embedding via `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` at cosine ≥0.75): micro P=0.652 R=0.460 F1=0.539, macro P=0.645 R=0.476 F1=0.534. **Caveat (CR-005 §3.1's required wording): FACE was supervised with 5-fold CV on the same book; this is zero/few-shot on held-out chapters — not a like-for-like comparison.** Against FACE's own published reference points (micro F1 0.76, macro F1 0.60; linguistic-only baseline micro F1 0.65; CopyRNN 0.23; MTurk humans 0.39): our lenient micro F1 (0.539) sits below FACE's supervised system and the linguistic-only baseline, comfortably above CopyRNN, and above the MTurk human baseline — a reasonable showing for zero/few-shot, not a strong one. **Recall by gold n-gram length is the clearest pattern: 1-gram 0.267, 2-gram 0.540, 3-gram 0.719, 4-gram 0.250** (the 4-gram bucket is only 8 gold concepts total, too small to read much into) — the system is systematically missing short, generic gold terms ("documents", "collections", "search", "terms", "index") while doing much better on longer, more specific phrases. **Precision by predicted role: defined 0.741, used 0.732, mentioned 0.439** — "mentioned"-role predictions are the noisiest, mostly longer descriptive noun phrases the annotators didn't select (e.g. "Enterprise, institutional, and domain-specific search", "Automatic document classification"). **Likely cause of the 1-gram recall gap:** `prompts/concept_extraction/v1.md`'s own guidance ("not a generic word (e.g. 'approach', 'case', 'example', 'issue')") plausibly over-filters short IR terms that FACE's annotators *did* mark as gold, even though they're generic-sounding out of context. 10 FP + 10 FN examples pulled for review (not reproduced here — see the scoring run). **Per CR-005 §5, this is a stop point: the owner may ask for one prompt revision (dev-split only) before continuing to the P&D run.**
- **2026-09-29 · 👤 CR-005 STOP 2 response: one dev-only prompt revision, run as an ablation (v1 / v2 / v2+g), selection rule fixed in advance.** Owner's diagnosis matched mine (1-gram recall gap, likely the generic-word rule over-filtering). `prompts/concept_extraction/v2.md`: (a) keeps excluding discourse/meta words ("approach", "case", "example", "issue") but explicitly stops excluding short domain terms just because they're everyday English words (networking examples only — "packet", "frame", "link", "host" — verified absent from the IIR dev gold set, so the dev ablation isn't contaminated by its own prompt); (b) requires a term, not a description — coordinated phrases ("X, Y and Z") must be split into separate concepts, not returned as one combined phrase. `prompts/concept_extraction_gleaning/v2.md` (the "+g" in "v2+g"): unlike v1's open-ended "look again" gleaning, v2 shows only the candidate noun phrases the main pass didn't select and asks specifically which of *those* are concepts — a targeted second pass, not another open search. `extract_concepts_for_section` gained `include_gleaning: bool` (v2 alone runs the main pass only, no gleaning call at all; v2+g adds the targeted gleaning pass) and always computes `unselected_candidate_terms` for the gleaning render (harmless unused kwarg for v1's template). Both v1 files untouched (CLAUDE.md rule 8). **Selection rule, fixed before seeing any ablation numbers:** highest lenient micro F1 on dev; if two versions are within 0.02, take the simpler (fewer LLM calls) one — so v1 beats v2 beats v2+g on ties, in that preference order. Ablation results and the IIR-test headline number are logged separately once run.

Precision by role also improved on "defined" (0.741→0.792) and "mentioned" (0.439→0.633), though "used" dropped (0.732→0.574) — v2 extracts more used-role concepts overall (higher recall) at a precision cost there specifically. **Selection: v2 vs v2+g differ by 0.0043 lenient micro F1, inside the fixed 0.02 tie threshold → the simpler version wins → v2 (no gleaning pass) is the chosen prompt**, not v2+g — the extra gleaning call isn't earning its cost once v2's main prompt already fixed the generic-word problem.

Cleared to run relations next.
- **2026-09-30 · 👤 CR-005 canonicalisation fix: three "same" merges were wrong (kind/instance collapsed into category) — fixed four ways before any further real spend.** Manual review of P&D's 223 "same" merges found 3 clear errors in a 20-item sample: `"High-Level Data Link Control (HDLC)"` merged into `"Synchronous Data Link Control (SDLC)"` (related but conventionally distinct protocol standards — the closest match to the owner's own "two distinct protocols merged" example); `"Internet"` merged into `"internetworking"` (an instance collapsed into the general mechanism); `"cyclic redundancy check"` merged into `"error-detecting code"` (a kind collapsed into its category). All three matter because a wrong "same" merge is silent: everything the source text says about the more specific/instance concept gets misattributed to the general one, with nothing downstream showing it happened (unlike a *missed* merge, which just leaves two visible near-duplicate nodes). Fixes, all committed with tests before any further real canonicalisation run:
  1. **Exact-string duplicates now auto-merge with no LLM call at all** (`ConceptRegistry.find_exact_match`, case-insensitive against name + aliases) — removes the trivial ~170-of-223 case (from the P&D breakdown: 170 exact-string dupes, 53 genuine LLM-recognised variants) as a source of LLM-decision noise, and is strictly cheaper.
  2. **`prompts/canonicalize/v2.md`**: "same" now means *interchangeable in any sentence of the book* only — a true synonym, abbreviation/expansion, or spelling/plural variant. Explicitly: if one is a kind, instance, part, version, predecessor/successor, or standardised variant of the other, the answer is never "same" — always narrower/broader/different. "When unsure, choose different" is stated as an explicit tie-break, with the asymmetric-cost reasoning above spelled out in the prompt itself. Examples are generic (laptop/computer, a monarch instance, engine/car, Windows 95/Windows, IMAP4/IMAP2) — none of the three flagged pairs appear in the prompt. v1 untouched (CLAUDE.md rule 8).
  3. **New `configs/canonical_overrides.yaml`** (`never_merge`/`force_merge`, checked by `canonicalize_mention` before any prompt version gets a say): the three flagged pairs are now in `never_merge`, filtered out of the candidate list before the LLM ever sees them (cheaper, not just safer — fewer candidates shown). `force_merge` exists for the opposite case but is empty for now.
  4. **narrower (146) / broader (38) decisions never merge** (confirmed unchanged behaviour: both mint a new concept node) **but are no longer silently discarded** — `Checkpoint.taxonomy_candidates` now logs `{concept_id, matched_concept_id, decision, reason, section_id}` for each, for the taxonomy layer (is_a / broader candidates) to consume later. Previously this information existed on `CanonicalizationOutcome` but `run_canonicalize_stage` only ever read it for the "same" case.

  New `CanonicalOverrides` (load/is_never_merge/is_force_merge, 2 tests), `ConceptRegistry.find_exact_match` (3 tests), `CanonicalizationOutcome` gained `auto_merged`/`overridden` flags, `PromptSet.load` now defaults `canonicalize_version="v2"` alongside the existing `concept_extraction_version="v2"`. New `src/cumap/eval/stats.py` (`wilson_ci`, Wilson score interval, 7 tests) for the merge-precision spot-check this fix calls for, and reused later for CR-005 §3.2's edge spot-check (same statistical need). 15 canonicalize tests (7 new), 6 pipeline tests (1 new), 253/253 project-wide, ruff clean. No real spend in this step — the P&D canonicalisation run (943 mentions) will be re-run for real next, expected cost ≤$1.50 per the owner's approval gate.

Only **43 non-trivial "same" merges** (LLM-decided, not exact-string) remain, presented to the owner for ok/wrong marking (CR-005 §3.2-style Wilson CI, labelled "owner check" — pending the owner's marks, not yet computed). **734 distinct concepts vs. the old 720** — v2 correctly refuses some merges v1 wrongly made, so slightly more (not fewer) concepts survive, as expected from tightening "same". **189 taxonomy candidates logged** (154 narrower, 35 broader) — real numbers from the new run, not directly comparable to the old run's 146/38 since the underlying decisions changed. - **2026-09-30 · CORRECTION to the IIR-test headline above: the 0.407 was measured on wrong/incomplete test text (my scraping error, not a model gap). The scraper is a scratch script, not yet in `src/` — worth promoting with tests if this benchmark is reused.

re-check against the STOP 4 merge sheet. *Comparison edges:* `contrasts_with` and `trades_off_with` must carry a `dimension` grounded in the evidence sentence, otherwise the pair is recorded as NO_RELATION (4 of 7 spot-check failures were lists read as contrasts); a test is added with the relation prompt. *Longest-match mentions are kept* as correct hygiene (it changes pair counts) but are not expected to fix spot-check failures: diagnostic (a) found 1 of 7 failures with a substring endpoint (vs 13 of 23 correct edges). *Merge marks* are read from `data/gold/merges/merges_owner_check.csv` (owner-copied). **STOP 2 pre-registration (fixed BEFORE any v3 run):** dev = IIR chapters 1-3 (13 sections); experiments E1 (condensed FACE code-book in our own words, networking examples only), E2 (3 deliberately varied runs; E2 is scored twice, union and >= 2-of-3 vote, and enters selection with whichever is higher on dev lenient micro F1), E3 (consistency propagation, $0 post-processing on a longest-match matcher, role `mentioned`, source `propagation`), E4 (1-3 few-shot examples from OTHER dev sections, leave-one-section-out; the examples are dev gold names, allowed since tuning is on dev), E5 (node_type + granularity rule); each run singly on top of v2, then the best combination. **Selection rule:** highest **lenient micro F1 on dev**; if two candidates are within 0.02, take the simpler (fewer calls per section, then fewer components). Reported for each: exact micro, lenient micro, macro, recall by n-gram length, precision by role. **Test:** v2 and the chosen v3 run once each on the full IIR test split (all sections passing the 90% gold-presence gate); **v3 exact micro F1 is the headline**, compared exact-to-exact with FACE. *E1 source:* the code-book is Table 1 of Wang, Chau, Thaker, Brusilovsky & He, arXiv 2005.11422 (May 2020), read from the paper itself: ten rules (noun phrases only; abbreviations of a concept are concepts; annotate the whole noun phrase but drop general adjectives; a combination of two concept phrases is a concept; split conjunctions; annotate all variants; domain-special phrases from related fields count; ignore an abbreviation in brackets; keep punctuation inside a term; well-known important examples count). Note a title/year mismatch to fix in citations: the arXiv preprint (2020) is titled "Concept Annotation for Intelligent Textbooks"; `data/raw/external/iir_face/SOURCE.md` cites "Knowledge Annotation for Intelligent Textbooks", Technology, Knowledge and Learning (2021), the published version.

- **2026-10-02 · CR-008: alias rules R0-R3, as built.** *Order:* R0 lexicon, R1 key, R2 acronym, R3-strong, then R4 (unchanged LLM path); the lexicon `different` list is a guard at every point. *R1:* NFKC + casefold, dashes/quotes, hyphen/space/punctuation collapsed, leading article, spelling map (`configs/alias_rules.yaml`), head-noun lemma, plural acronyms ("BBUs"; the shared lemmatiser skips "-us", found by a test). *Acronym-guard word list* (all-caps vs lower-case twin): as, can, is, it, or, be, do, so, no, an, at, by, in, on, to, up, us, we, he, me, my, if, am, are, was, will, may, mac, pan, lan, ram, sip, tap, bus. *R2:* Schwartz-Hearst detection with stricter validity than the paper (short form must have >= 2 capitals or a capital plus digit; the long form may not contain digits, "=" or the short form itself; reverse "SF (long form)" requires the whole parenthetical to be the long form), because a first draft produced junk pairs ("that is", "SWP would call send"). A name that embeds its acronym ("exclusive OR (XOR)") is split with an ordered-letters check (S-H alone rejects it). Ambiguous acronyms (one short form, >= 2 long forms) are scoped to the chapters where each pairing is defined. *R3 strong:* "also known as", "also called", "abbreviated", "or simply". *R3 weak (review only):* "sometimes called", "often referred to", "or alternatively", "or called", "officially goes by". The pattern "X is called Y" was **dropped**: it only finds ordinary definitions ("the variation in latency is called jitter"). R3 requires X and Y to be whole noun phrases (spaCy POS check); without it the first backtest proposed "path = LTE" and "networks = Wi-Fi". *Backtest (STOP 1):* R1 12 and R2 25 of the owner's "same" pairs, no owner-marked wrong pair merged by any rule; 40 owner-"same" pairs stay with R4. *Alternatives:* spaCy `AbbreviationDetector` (extra dependency; our 50 lines suffice and are testable).

- **2026-10-02 · CR-008 item 2 (`corrects_intuition`): diagnosis (fix approved the same day, see the next entry).** All 4 flagged edges (of 327) were read. Two are real warnings the flag attached to the wrong edge (MTU "Note that this value is smaller than the largest packet size"; "Central Offices ... despite their names implying centralized"), one is study advice ("it is tempting to settle for understanding the way it's done today"), one is a scope note ("Note that this particular implementation does not support piggybacking"). Cause: the **prompt/schema, not code**: (a) the qualifier prompt lists "note that ... does not" as a trigger, so remarks fire it; (b) a warning is a property of a passage, but it is asked per pair, so it lands on whichever pair the registry kept; (c) nothing verifies the free-text `intuition`. Proposed fix: qualifier prompt v4 and schema drop `corrects_intuition`/`intuition` (code only copied the field); the misconception stage becomes the only producer; add the cues "despite" and "Note that" to `misconception_cues.yaml` (structuring filters the noise at about $0.02 per candidate). Old edges keep the field in the data, ignored.

Fixed in one line (test `test_add_new_keeps_the_mentions_source`); the true counts come from the raw per-section mentions: 735 propagation mentions in the CR-007 run, 575 forward and 144 backward, which matches the backfill detector (35 calls for 145 unrecorded earlier occurrences). M2 was tightened for precision (a span that is itself a known whole term never fires; clause verbs do not extend a compound; the longest term-like candidate wins). - **2026-10-03 · CR-009 STOP 2 pre-registration (written before any experiment ran).** *Selection metric:* exact micro F1 on IIR dev (13 sections), scored with the existing FACE scorer; a mention is scored like a new concept (the surface form, the node name or an alias, whichever matches gold). *Tie rule:* a candidate within 0.02 of the best goes to the simpler arm (fewer calls, then fewer components). *Order and arms:* (1) **G1 vs G2**, each with the verifier, max_iterations 3, no pruner (live); (2) **max_iterations in {1, 2, 3}** on the step-1 winner (replay of stored iterations: iteration k's verified output, flagged items dropped, unresolved M1 not counted); (3) **M4 on/off** on the step-2 configuration (live); (4) **M5 on/off** with the step-3 choice (live; added at the owner's request); (5) **pruner P1 tau:** leave-one-chapter-out models score each chapter, tau = the highest exact micro F1 among values in {0.1, ..., 0.9} whose recall drops by <= 0.01 relative to no pruner, else tau = 0 (replay); (6) the **V2 LLM concept verifier is deferred** (not built; CR-009 §14 lists it first to drop) and **P2 is not built** (no `peft`). The chosen configuration is then run once end to end on dev; its numbers are the reported dev numbers. *Reported, never selected:* B0 = v3 (E3, exact micro 0.514 on dev), iteration 0 of the step-1 winner (raw generator output), and L0 (iteration 0 with the rules applied offline: auto-fixes, flagged items dropped, M1 hints written as mentions, M2 spans replaced, no re-prompt). *Settings fixed now:* rho 1.5, retriever k 30 and cap 120, hints may be rejected with a reason, carry-forward on, one tier (bulk) in every iteration, prompt `concept_generator/v4`, bank approved. The test split is run once, after STOP 2 selection, and never used for selection.

- **2026-10-05 · CR-010 STOP 2, IIR dev results (13 sections, `reports/cr010_stop2_dev.md`).** *Baseline:* N0 re-run live at the frozen configuration scored exact micro F1 **0.474** (P 0.435 / R 0.522), not the 0.505 reported for the CR-009 dev FINAL: the same configuration moves by about 0.03 F1 between live runs (the generator is not deterministic and a changed store changes the cached prompts), which is larger than every arm's gain below; the paired section bootstrap does not capture this generator-sampling variance. *Arms (post-passes over the N0 run, paired on the same sections):* **N1** 0.490 (+0.016, bootstrap 95% [+0.005, +0.027], precision drop -0.003, rescue precision 19/35 exact, one-token recall 0.547 -> 0.605, recovered 15 of 232 residual gold terms, cost $0.027); **N2** (theta 3.0, 5/13 sections resampled) 0.485 (+0.011, [+0.002, +0.022]); **N3** 0.477 (+0.003, [-0.005, +0.012]). *Rule outcome:* N1 and N2 qualify through the positive bootstrap bound (neither reaches +0.02); they are within the 0.02 tie band, so the simpler arm, **N1, is selected on dev**. Candidate generation is the ceiling: only 44 of the 232 residual gold terms appear in N1's candidate list. *Implementation decisions made before the first scored live run (no gold used):* candidate caps are per class (20 one-token / 10 multi-token) so N3 is exactly N1 minus its one-token class (a shared cap of 30 let one-token terms crowd out nearly every phrase); candidate coverage is exact-key (a recorded 'inverted index' does not hide 'index'); stop words are trimmed from candidate ends. *Defect fixed:* `concept_rescue` had the default 1000 `max_output_tokens`, so all 13 first N1 calls failed with truncated JSON (an infrastructure failure, not evidence); set to 6000 in `configs/default.yaml` and N1/N3 re-run. *Interpretation choices (mine, recorded):* the CR's 'paired section-level bootstrap lower bound' is the 2.5th percentile of the 10,000-resample distribution (seed 20261005); the N2 trigger (items per 100 words in N0's output below theta) is frozen as the grid value with the highest dev exact micro F1, ties to the smaller theta: my first draft applied the 0.02 tie band to theta as well, which would have picked a theta that never fires, so it was removed before theta was frozen, theta = **3.0** written to `n2.trigger_frozen` before any test run. *Spend:* $0.17 of the $5 STOP 2 cap. *Limits:* 13 dev sections are one book part (chapters 1-3); the held-out test split (70 sections) is still unused by CR-010 and has been used once for CR-009 reporting.

No arm is adopted; the N1/N0 decision is returned to Research.** *Exact micro F1 (P / R / F1):* frozen CR-009 N0 0.447 / 0.569 / **0.501**; **N1 (confirmatory)** 0.444 / 0.599 / **0.510**, Δ +0.009, paired bootstrap 95% [+0.005, +0.014], precision change -0.003, one-token recall 0.696 -> 0.741, rescue precision 126/270 exact; N3 (descriptive) 0.451 / 0.591 / **0.512**, Δ +0.011 [+0.007, +0.014], precision +0.004, rescue precision 83/134; N2 (descriptive, theta 3.0, 14/70 sections resampled) 0.442 / 0.583 / **0.503**, Δ +0.002 [-0.000, +0.005]; fresh N0 replication (sensitivity check) 0.433 / 0.553 / **0.486**. *Against the pre-registered criteria (+0.02 or bootstrap lower bound > 0, precision drop <= 0.02):* N1 **meets them through the positive lower bound, not through the +0.02 threshold**; N3 meets them; N2 does not. *Contradictions and cautions, all reported:* (1) the effect is smaller than on dev (+0.016) and is under +0.02; (2) **the descriptive N3 (one-token hints disabled) did at least as well as N1 on test (+0.011 vs +0.009, higher precision), reversing the dev ordering (+0.003 vs +0.016)**: the one-token class adds recall (one-token recall +0.045) but also 57 more 'inside a gold term' false positives (279 -> 336 vs 288 for N3), so the dev inference that one-token hints drive the gain is not supported; under the tie-band rule applied to the descriptive arms N3 would be preferred as the simpler arm; (3) **baseline instability:** the fresh N0 replication scored 0.486, 0.015 below the frozen run (paired 95% [-0.028, -0.002]); the arms are post-passes over the frozen run, so N1's gain is +0.009 against the frozen N0 but +0.024 [+0.012, +0.037] against the fresh replication (not item-paired), i.e. the size of the benefit depends on which baseline draw is the comparator, and the historical 0.501 may be a favourable draw; (4) candidate generation is still the ceiling: 193 of 1,262 exact-unmatched gold terms occur in N1's candidate list, 77 were recovered. *Execution:* attempt 1 was lost to a network outage (N1 70/70 calls failed, N0 replication 4/70 sections and 129/300 backfill calls failed; no metric computed from it, files kept under `data/processed/cr010/test/failed_attempt1_connection_outage/`); attempt 2 re-ran the identical frozen commands with 0 failed calls in every output (70/70 sections and 320 backfill calls for the replication, 70/70 for N1 and N3, 14/14 N2 samples). *Cost:* total CR-010 spend to date **$1.05** of the $5 STOP 2 cap (the N0 replication alone cost $0.63: the preflight estimate of $0.17-0.34 omitted the chapter-end backfill calls, 463 live calls in the replication). *Limits:* one split, one generated draw per arm; the bootstrap resamples sections only and does not capture generator-sampling variance (3 above); tuning was on dev only and the test split had already been used once for CR-009 reporting.
- **2026-10-05 · CR-010 STOP 3: eRST inventory audit (`reports/cr010_stop3_audit.json` / `.md`; $0, no model).** *Built:* `src/cumap/erst/` as a SEPARATE inventory from the domain `RelationRegistry` (registry loader that fails closed on unknown labels, `ErstEdge`/`ErstSignal` schemas, KG-effect guards, `python -m cumap.erst.audit`); `configs/erst_relations.yaml` (the package file, unchanged) and `configs/erst_signals.yaml` (new: the signal taxonomy transcribed from the paper's Table 1). *Source check:* the primary paper (arXiv:2403.13560v2, kept locally in `data/raw/external/erst_paper/`, not committed) was read directly: its Appendix A Table A.1 has **32 rows**, and the package inventory equals it exactly in **labels (32 = 32, none missing, none extra) and nuclearity symbols (32/32)**; the nuclearity legend, the statement that SAME-UNIT is not a discourse relation, the seven non-DM signal types (graphical, lexical, morphological, numerical, reference, semantic, syntactic) with every subtype of Table 1, and the rule that secondary edges express relations and not prominence all agree with the package files. *Counts:* 32 labels, 31 true discourse relations, technical SAME-UNIT; 14 discourse coarse classes (+ the package's `technical` bucket); nuclearity symbols →← 19, Λ 7, ← 3, → 3. *Result:* 21 checks, **0 failed**, 1 flag. *Design points:* SAME-UNIT is never an edge or a classifier choice (the choice set is the 31 relations + `NO_ERST_RELATION`); the package files SAME-UNIT under a `technical` bucket rather than a coarse class `SAME` (the naming rule `<coarse>-<fine>` binds the 31 discourse relations only); an eRST label never creates a domain edge by itself (a domain edge needs independent domain-semantic evidence; ORGANIZATION-* is document structure only; RESTATEMENT-* may queue a same-concept candidate only; JOINT-LIST never yields contrasts_with/trades_off_with; CONTINGENCY-CONDITION never yields prerequisite_of; the contrast grounded-dimension requirement stays); a semantic signal (synonymy, meronymy, antonymy, repetition) never licenses a domain relation or a merge; `equivalent_to` stays retired. *Flags for Research:* (1) **orientation reading is an inference:** the paper's legend says '←' is for satellite relations that only go left-to-right; I read the arrow as pointing at the nucleus ('←' nucleus first, satellite after; '→' satellite first), which fits the definitions of all six fixed-orientation rows (elaboration and partial restatement follow their nucleus; heading, preparation and question precede it) but is not stated in the paper in those words; `ErstEdge` enforces it for primary edges only. (2) **Existing CR-008 guard gap, not changed here:** `Lexicon.is_different` recognises an entry only by its exact surface forms: the lexicon holds HDLC/SDLC as 'High-Level Data Link Control (HDLC)' / 'Synchronous Data Link Control (SDLC)', so bare `HDLC` vs `SDLC` is NOT recognised as different (Internet/internetworking and CRC/error-detecting code are); options: the owner adds the bare forms to the approved entry, or CR-008 matching also compares the short form of an 'X (ABC)' entry, or leave it because node names carry the long form. (3) the package definitions paraphrase the paper (median token overlap 0.62; lowest ELABORATION-ADDITIONAL 0.21, CONTEXT-BACKGROUND 0.30, EVALUATION-COMMENT 0.31; all three read as faithful paraphrases). (4) the paper copy is arXiv v2; the journal version was not compared. *Tests:* 36 new, 562 in all, ruff clean.

Accepted edges are drawn round-robin over relations so rare relations appear (15 relations, 1-10 per relation), so the sheet is relation-balanced, not proportional. The blind sheet has exactly the package template columns, only the five evidence fields filled, items and the A/B concept order randomised; the model-side fields live in a separate `MANIFEST_KEY_DO_NOT_SHARE` file; the NOT-GOLD mapping table was never read. (2) the frozen cue score tests cues by substring ('for' in 'information', 'so' in 'also'): 71.7% of all P&D sentences are cue sentences (54.0% with word boundaries), so it is a weak ranking signal; P0 is frozen and was not changed. *Definitions I had to choose, flagged for Research:* P1 (the same-sentence enumeration is already complete, so P1 = P0 + the pairs the budget left out); P2 'high-confidence' cue (word-boundary match on relation-bearing cues, function words for/so/if/but/then/when/while/since/after/before/until/next/once and the copula 'is a' excluded); 'sufficiently represented' relation (not defined by the CR; 8 of 15 relations have >= 5 test items). *Tests:* 14 new, 576 in all, ruff clean. *Awaiting:* the owner's single annotation of the 180 rows and approval of the pair-recall sampling spend.

- **2026-10-09 · Dataset snapshot rule adopted (owner).** Before replacing, upgrading, reformatting or switching a dataset used by an active CR: archive branch `archive/<cr>-<dataset>-pre-change` + annotated tag at the exact HEAD (never modified later); for gitignored / local-only / licence-restricted data a separate local archive with SHA-256 and a manifest record (source, version, commit, archive location, branch/tag, per-file hashes, redistribution restriction, never pushed if prohibited); a branch or tag is not a dataset backup unless the files are tracked; continue on the original CR branch; every result names its dataset snapshot; verify the pre-change commit and exact bytes (or an authoritative reproducible source) before any destructive replacement. Recorded in CLAUDE.md. No dataset change is pending: SciNLP is still the first download (`e78342be517e`); its manifest now carries a `dataset_snapshot` entry (no archive yet).

- **2026-10-09 · 👤 CR-012 STOP 1 PASS: Research rulings D1-D4 recorded; STOP 2 authorised (no held-out model evaluation).** *D1:* SciNLP test (3 documents) gets micro P/R/F1 over the 3 docs, per-document P/R/F1, macro mean, min/max document F1 and `CI_status = NOT_EVALUABLE_SMALL_N`; no entity/edge bootstrap, no dev+test pooling; a software-diagnostic 3-document bootstrap is labelled `DESCRIPTIVE_SMALL_N_ONLY` and is never the headline; the generic document bootstrap is built and validated on non-held-out data; P&D-E2E keeps section-level bootstrap 95% intervals. *D2:* SciNLP has no identity gold, so canonical identity gold / E2 / canonical-node F1 / pairwise identity F1 / B-cubed / C0-C1 attribution are `NOT_SUPPORTED`; strict MENTION-LEVEL scoring ("CR-012 SciNLP strict scorer", not the official scorer): entity = exact source-text span (+ type for typed), relation = exact source and target spans, endpoint types for Rel+, relation, direction unless symmetric; predicted canonical nodes are projected back to their evidence-grounded mentions; identity clusters are never inferred from strings or aliases. SciNLP oracles: E0, E1 (gold spans + types), E2 not supported, E3-M (gold mention endpoint pairs, relation label hidden), E4-M (correct gold pair, classifier predicts relation / direction); E3-M minus E1 is called post-mention / endpoint-delivery loss, not pure pair-selection loss. P&D-E2E keeps E0-E4. Paper baselines are context only (release, split and scorer differ). *D3:* linear budget `B(u) = min(candidate_pool_size, max(1, ceil(14.2 * words / 1000)))` per document (SciNLP) or section (P&D), existing frozen ranking and coverage policy, no benchmark-specific ranking; per-unit logging of words, eligible pairs, budget, selected pairs, saturation, gold-edge pair recall; E3 forces gold endpoint pairs (relation hidden) and measures pair-selection loss. *D4:* soft warning $40, hard cap $50 for the mandatory scope only (SciNLP, P&D-E2E, oracles, stability repeats); optional SciERC / SciER / OSKGC / Text2KGBench not authorised; byte-identical calls may reuse cache across E0/E1/E3/E4, stability repeats must not reuse outputs; refresh the preflight after STOP 2/3 and return before STOP 4 if the mandatory projection exceeds $50. *SciNLP release freeze:* `SciNLP-repo-e78342be` (AKADDC/SciNLP, 60 documents, 51/6/3, 6,429 entities, 1,649 relations); the paper/repository count discrepancy (7,072 / 1,826) is recorded and never mixed into results; `KG_triples.txt` excluded. *CR-011:* `CR011_PRE_POST = NOT_APPLICABLE_NO_ADOPTED_TRANSFORMATION`; no delta_F1_sleep = 0 is reported; the pre/post chart is NOT_APPLICABLE; the baseline stays the unchanged `slice3_c2`. Frozen eval config: `configs/cr012_e2e_eval.yaml`.

## 2026-10-10 · CR-012 STOP 3 continuation: grounded converter, SciNLP rehearsal, freeze
- Decision: graph-to-score conversion uses evidence-grounded per-node mention spans (`production_graph.grounded_spans`), also for SciNLP prediction; the global string-match converter is superseded (invalid_runs.md). `build_qualifiers_v3` tolerates a registry with no `action_type` qualifier (default path identical). Cost constants refreshed from measured pilot oracle and SciNLP dev rates. Evaluation code/config frozen (`configs/cr012_e2e_eval.yaml` version `stop3-frozen`, `frozen_hashes.json` final_freeze).
- Reason: shared surfaces ("bandwidth") were wrongly counted as false merges and removed gold edges from denominators; the benchmark registry has no CUM-only qualifiers.
- Alternatives: keep global matching (rejected: violates rule 24); add an `action_type` stub to the SciNLP registry (rejected: invents benchmark schema).

## 2026-10-14 · CR-017 STOP 4: historical replay and SciNLP scope
- **Decision:** replay the unified evaluator over frozen CR-012 artefacts (`cumap.cr017.replay`, 0 model calls, 118 frozen files hashed before/after, 0 changed). P&D held-out values for strict F1, concept F1 and pair recall equal the frozen CR-012 numbers exactly at E0–E4. SciNLP is reported separately with mention-level analogues only (entity strict as concept analogue, reached/endpoint-ok pair recall, relation strict); hierarchy `NOT_EVALUABLE` (no identity gold); Relation Macro F1 `NOT_COMPUTED`, flagged for a Research decision.
- **Test guard:** `tests/test_cr012_stop1.py` isolation list now allows `cr017` (scoring-only, builds no prompts, calls no model), alongside `cr012`/`explain`.
- **Reason:** compatibility check without inventing gold; SciNLP has no per-class classifier outcomes at mention level.
- **Alternatives:** defining SciNLP macro F1 silently (rejected); pooling with P&D (forbidden).

## 2026-10-15 · Explorer: plain-language wording, cleaner layout, P/R on scorecard cards (on the CR-018 branch at the owner's request)
- **Decision:** reworded the KG explorer front end (`kg_explorer.html`, `kg_explorer_trace.js`, `kg_explorer_eval.js`) for readers with no prior knowledge. Dataset names stay "P&D dataset" / "SciNLP dataset"; modes are "How well it scores" and "Simple / Detailed"; "gold" is shown as "human answer key"; failure codes, E0-E4 levels, CI statuses and scorecard metrics get one-line plain explanations, and the original codes/terms stay visible as small tags or tooltips (HEADLINE / DIAGNOSTICS / AUDIT, REPLAYED, RULE / MODEL, NOT_EVALUABLE). For Relation Macro F1, precision and recall are the mean of the per-class values over classes with gold support, a class with no predictions counting as precision 0 (the macro-F1 zero-fill rule).
- **Unchanged:** every number, all payloads, the frozen CR-013 explanation templates (rule 33) and the evaluation codes' frozen meanings; the trace layer still contains no evaluation vocabulary; production views stay free of evaluation text.

## 2026-10-15 · CR-019 pre-registration (written before any test-split rescoring)
- **Decision:** primary SciNLP variant `IDX-P1` (occurrence index + the edge's own evidence sentence; unplaced edges count as one FP each); secondary `FROZEN-UNPLACED-FP` and `IDX-P3`; P&D variant `IDX` (CR-017 five metrics with indexed node spans); N0 split into `N0-absent` / `N0-unrecorded` as an added field. Shared surfaces are owned by the node whose recorded evidence windows contain the occurrence, otherwise unassigned (keeps the CR-012 `invalid_runs.md` fix). The index never writes mentions (rule 24 unchanged). Frozen CR-012 / CR-017 numbers stay frozen and are printed beside every variant.
- **Reason:** on SciNLP train/dev the answer key is 100% intra-sentence; projection ceilings P1 0.903 / 0.955 and P3 0.900 / 0.967 (train / dev) tie within 0.02, so the simpler, evidence-preserving P1 is primary; ±1 / ±3-sentence windows fall to 0.649 / 0.471 (train) and are rejected.
- **Alternatives:** paragraph-proximity projection (rejected by the ceilings); rewriting rule 24 so string occurrences become mentions (rejected: the index is a separate layer).

## 2026-10-15 · CR-019 STOP 1 answered by the owner; STOP 2 approved with a $25 cap
- **Decision (STOP 1, Research/owner "yes to both"):** `IDX-P1` is reported as the SciNLP protocol beside the frozen CR-012 numbers (frozen stays frozen); `IDX-P3` is kept as a secondary figure.
- **Decision (STOP 2, owner):** build and run Stage B; hard spending cap **$25** for the whole stage (sum of `spend_from_log` over every Stage B run id), on top of rule 7 (dry run, `--limit 20`, full).
- **Stage B pre-registration (written before any Stage B call):** arm `MULTI` = relation family v4 / choice v5 / qualifiers v5, which see a PASSAGE of up to **K = 3** sentences in which both concepts occur (the pair's original sentence first, then the others in text order; production splitter and longest-match matcher; same section only); the evidence quote must come from one sentence. Everything else is held fixed: same concepts, same selected pairs (copied from the baseline run), same tier, same checks. Baseline arm `SINGLE` = the unchanged production relation stage. Data: SciNLP **dev** (6 papers; new E0 runs `cr012_scinlp_cr019dev_*_E0`) and the P&D development sections 1.5 and 2.6 (existing `cr012_pilot_*_E0` runs as SINGLE). Primary metric: SciNLP dev `IDX-P1` strict relation F1 (pooled); secondary: SciNLP dev `IDX-P3`, P&D pilot Strict E2E Edge F1 and Relation Macro F1. **Recommendation rule:** recommend MULTI if SciNLP dev IDX-P1 F1 rises by >= 0.02 and P&D pilot Strict E2E F1 does not fall by more than 0.01; otherwise keep SINGLE. Test papers and P&D held-out sections are not touched by Stage B.

## 2026-10-15 · CR-019 Stage B result: KEEP_SINGLE (pre-registered rule)
- **Result ($6.49 of the $25 cap; `reports/cr019/stage_b.md`):** SciNLP dev (6 papers) IDX-P1 strict relation F1 SINGLE 0.096 -> MULTI 0.086 (Δ -0.010; IDX-P3 0.114 -> 0.085); P&D development sections 1.5 / 2.6 Strict E2E Edge F1 0.086 -> 0.112 (Δ +0.025), Relation Macro F1 0.600 -> 0.686. Rule (MULTI only if SciNLP dev rises >= 0.02 and P&D dev falls <= 0.01) gives **KEEP_SINGLE**; the production relation stage is unchanged.
- **Reading:** more context makes the classifier accept more edges (SciNLP 132 -> 145 edges, more `other`) at lower precision on scientific papers, while it helps on the textbook, where 24% of development gold relations span sentences. Small samples (2 P&D sections, 179 SciNLP dev relations); not evidence either way for the held-out sets, which Stage B did not touch.
- **Known limitation:** "one sentence per line" in prompt v4/v5 is not exact when a P&D sentence contains line breaks (the cap of 3 sentences holds; every MULTI quote was checked to lie in one line). Not fixed in place (rule 8: the versions have saved results).
- **Alternatives for a later CR:** MULTI for textbook (P&D) runs only; a passage that adds only cross-sentence context when the first sentence is a list.

## 2026-10-15 · CR-019 follow-up diagnostic: spelling variants are a MATCHING gap, not a merge failure (no adoption yet)
- **Finding:** SciNLP "prefixtuning" / "Prefix tuning" are unmatched because two equivalences disagree: the canonicalisation R1 key (`alias_rules.r1_key`, hyphen/space/case collapsed) treats them as the same name, the rule-17 text matcher (`MentionMatcher`, token + lemma) does not. "prefixtuning" is a GROBID de-hyphenation artifact (11 times in 2021.acl-long.353); the generator records about one surface per concept per chunk, so no alias is created. Node merging itself works: across 9 SciNLP runs no two nodes share an R1 key except one acronym clash (`rp`).
- **Prototype ($0):** `cr019/occurrences.R1KeyMatcher` + `build_index(matching="r1key")` + variants `IDXR1-P1` / `IDXR1-P3`; default matching unchanged. Two prototype bugs fixed during diagnosis: article-initial windows (found on SciNLP dev) and short-acronym forms compared against one form only (found on the test papers: it only restored the token matcher's existing behaviour, but it was seen on test, so the test figures below are not clean held-out evidence).
- **Measured:** SciNLP dev gold mentions owned 429 -> 433 (16 new matches, 4 gold; 3 lost); test 613 -> 632 (31 new, 20 gold; 3 lost), concept F1 0.611 -> 0.622, IDX-P3 relation F1 0.332 -> 0.362, IDX-P1 0.195 unchanged; P&D held-out concept 0.656 -> 0.655, strict and pair recall unchanged; prefix-tuning variants in 2021.acl-long.353 owned 74 of 76 (the 2 left are an answer-key span "prefixtuning ( bottom )" and a different phrase).
- **Open:** whether to adopt R1-key matching in the occurrence index (evaluation), in production matching (rule 17 amendment), and/or as deterministic R1-variant aliases; owner decision.

## 2026-10-15 · CR-020 opened (owner: "do option 2"): R1-key matching in production, pre-registered
- **Decision:** `MentionMatcher` gains `mode` (`token` default, `r1key`), process-wide via `CUMAP_MATCHING`; frozen scorers pin `token`. Experiment and non-inferiority adoption rule as in `docs/change-requests/CR-020-r1key-matching.md` §3-4 (SciNLP dev IDXR1-P1 F1 drop <= 0.005 and P&D dev Strict E2E drop <= 0.01 -> adopt). Concepts are not re-run; pair generation, selection and relations are. Cap $10. Same branch as CR-019 (owner precedent).
- **Reason:** one name equivalence (R1) for both merging and text matching; spelling variants were invisible to the pipeline.
- **Alternatives:** evaluation-only matching (option 1) or deterministic R1-variant aliases (option 3), not chosen by the owner.

## 2026-10-15 · CR-020 result: ADOPT_R1KEY (pre-registered non-inferiority rule)
- **Result ($0.11 of the $10 cap; `reports/cr020/results.md`):** determinism check passed on all 8 dev runs (a TOKEN re-selection reproduces each base selection exactly); R1KEY changed 17 selected pairs in total. SciNLP dev IDXR1-P1 strict relation F1 TOKEN 0.096 -> R1KEY **0.120** (+0.023; precision 0.114 -> 0.138, recall 0.084 -> 0.106), IDXR1-P3 0.113 -> 0.133, pair-recall analogue 0.671 -> 0.714, concept F1 unchanged (0.433); P&D development sections identical on all five metrics. Rule: adopt.

The code default (`DEFAULT_MATCHING`) stays `token`, so scorers, replays and older runs are unchanged; a run made with r1key records `matching_mode` in `selection_stats`, and the CR-013 pair-pool replay uses the recorded mode. Rule 17 amended in CLAUDE.md. `configs/default.yaml` is not touched (its hash is frozen in the CR-011 handoff).
