## 2026-10-15 · CR-021 opened (owner: "do option 3"): promote only rejected links that name a MORE SPECIFIC term; switch off pending review
- **Decision:** built `more_specific` + `ConceptRun._promote` behind `configs/concept_policy.yaml` `promote_rejected_links: false`. Adoption rule (pre-registered): owner marks the 33 rule-selected rejected links; adopt if the "distinct concept" share has a Wilson 95% lower bound >= 0.70, then measure on a new-run-id re-run before any graph is replaced.
- **Reason:** of 140 rejected links (P&D production 124, SciNLP 16), 33 are more specific terms (adapter-tuning, File Transfer Protocol, physical link); 32 are shortened forms that would split one concept, and 75 are different words (often generic). The dev data cannot measure the change (P&D development sections 0 cases, SciNLP dev 2), so a human review is the honest test (rule 2 / 👤).
- **Alternatives:** promote every rejected link (rejected: splits and generic nodes); a paid dev experiment now (rejected: no signal).

## 2026-10-15 · CR-021 STOP 1 result: switch stays OFF (owner marks `data/gold/cr021_rejected_links_review_marked.csv`)
- **Result:** of the 33 rule-selected rejected links, 32 are marked "distinct concept", which passes the pre-registered criterion literally (0.97, Wilson LB 0.85). But 7 of those 32 are also marked "same as target" (backbone networks/backbone, IP datagrams/datagram, network links/link, HDLC protocol/High-Level Data Link Control, IEEE 802.11/802.11, source host/source, detection of collisions/collision detection), so promoting them would split a concept. Correct promotions (distinct AND not the same as the target) are 25/33 = 0.76, Wilson LB **0.59 < 0.70**. The criterion was worded ambiguously by the implementer (it should have been "distinct and not the same as the target"); the meaningful reading fails, so the switch is not turned on.
- **Over all 140 rejected links:** 88 (63%) new concepts, 47 (34%) the same as the target (the strict G-link check wrongly rejected an alias), 5 not concepts. The name-based kinds do not separate them (more specific 25/7/1, shortened 23/7/2, different words 40/33/2), so the rule also misses 63 of the 88 new concepts.
- **Open (owner):** an adjudicator that can tell "new concept" from "same as target" (e.g. a measured LLM arm using these 140 marks as gold, split into development and held-out halves before use; CR-009 rule 25 keeps the rule-based verifier as production until such an arm is measured), or keep CR-009 behaviour.


## 2026-10-15 · CR-021 option 1 (owner): measured LLM adjudicator for rejected links — pre-registration
- **Gold:** `data/gold/cr021_rejected_links_review_marked.csv`, 140 rows, **model-assisted and checked by the owner** (owner answer 2026-10-15; recorded as such in every report). Label per row: `new_concept` (distinct = yes and same-as-target = no), `same_as_target` (same-as-target = yes), `not_a_concept` (distinct = no and same = no). Caveat: labels drafted with a model's help may share an LLM adjudicator's biases, so agreement can be optimistic.
- **Split (fixed before any call):** stratified by label, rows ordered by SHA-256 of `cr021|run_id|section_id|surface|rejected_target` and alternated into `dev` / `heldout` (70 / 70). Prompt development uses `dev` only; `heldout` is scored once with the version chosen on `dev`.
- **Adjudicator:** task `rejected_link_adjudicator`, strong tier, Structured Outputs, decision in {new_concept, same_as_target, not_a_concept} plus an evidence quote that must be an exact substring of the evidence (rule 3; a failed quote counts as no decision, i.e. today's behaviour). Cap **$3**; rule 7 (dry run, `--limit 20`, full).
- **Adoption rule (held-out):** adopt adjudicated PROMOTION if the precision of `new_concept` decisions has a Wilson 95% lower bound >= 0.70 AND at most 20% of gold `same_as_target` rows are decided `new_concept`. Adopt adjudicated ALIASING (record a wrongly rejected link as a mention + alias) only if the precision of `same_as_target` decisions has a Wilson 95% lower bound >= 0.70. Otherwise the switch stays off.
- **Amendment before any held-out call (2026-10-15):** (a) the sheet lists one item twice ("retransmits" -> "retransmission", same section and evidence); the alternation had put the copies in different splits, so the held-out copy is moved to dev with its twin (no other row changes split; held-out = 68 rows). (b) Version selection on dev: v1 met the promotion rule on dev (new_concept precision 0.902, Wilson LB 0.775; 2/24 same rows decided new) but not aliasing (same_as_target precision 0.778, LB 0.592; main error: a narrower term decided same_as_target). One more version (v2) may be tried on dev; the version with the higher dev accuracy is scored on held-out, v1 on a tie.

## 2026-10-16 · CR-021 adjudicator ADOPTED for new production runs (pre-registered rule, held-out)
- **Result ($0.59 of the $3 cap; `reports/cr021/adjudicator.md`):** v2 chosen on dev (accuracy 0.861 vs v1 0.833 on the same 72 rows). Held-out, scored once (68 rows): accuracy 0.941; new_concept precision 42/43 = 0.977, Wilson LB **0.879** (>= 0.70); gold same-as-target rows decided new_concept 1/22 = 4.5% (<= 20%); same_as_target precision 20/21 = 0.952, Wilson LB **0.773** (>= 0.70). Both the promotion and the aliasing rule pass.
- **Adoption:** `configs/concept_policy.yaml` `rejected_link_policy: adjudicate` for NEW production runs; `ConceptRun._adjudicate` calls the strong tier only after the rule-based G-link check rejects a link (new_concept -> own node or the existing node with that name; same_as_target -> mention + alias; not_a_concept or a failed quote -> dropped as before), logging `G-link-adjudicated` with decision, reason and input hash. Schema and adopted version live in `concepts_v4/rejected_links.py`. CLAUDE.md rule 25 amended. The name-only "more specific" rule stays off.
- **Caveats:** the gold is model-assisted and owner-checked, so agreement may be optimistic; held-out accuracy is higher than dev (0.941 vs 0.861), within the variance of 68-72 rows; existing graphs change only when re-run (P&D `slice3_c2` has 124 rejected links, so a re-run would add about 124 strong-tier calls, roughly $0.30).

## 2026-10-16 · Rebuild results (`reports/cr021/rebuild.md`, $8.26: P&D $7.05 of $15, SciNLP $1.21 of $8)
- **SciNLP test (final-reporting, owner-approved):** strict relation IDXR1-P1 0.195 -> 0.147, IDXR1-P3 0.362 -> 0.333, frozen-scorer 0.050 -> 0.044; concept F1 (IDXR1) 0.622 -> 0.654. **SciNLP dev:** IDXR1-P1 0.096 -> 0.109, IDXR1-P3 0.113 -> 0.122. **P&D `slice3_c3` vs `slice3_c2`:** concepts 1,243 -> 1,351, aliases 188 -> 239, edges 529 -> 518; adjudicator decided 147 rejected links (77 new concept, 27 same as target, 43 not a concept); on the development sections 1.5 / 2.6: Concept F1 0.552 -> 0.548, Pair Recall 0.227 -> 0.257, Relation Macro F1 0.726 -> 0.538, Strict E2E 0.129 -> 0.140.
- **Reading:** a full re-run changes more than the two settings: once the adjudicator adds or merges a node, the generator sees different cards in later units, so concepts, pairs and relations diverge from the old run, and model non-determinism outside the cache adds noise. The before/after differences therefore mix the intended change with run-to-run variation, and they point in different directions (test down, dev up). A same-settings re-run would be needed to separate the two. `slice3_c2` stays the production graph (owner decision); the frozen CR-012 numbers are unchanged.
- **Example:** in 2021.acl-long.353 the adjudicator decided "adapter-tuning" is the same concept as the node "adapter" (the paper labels the method ADAPTER) and recorded it as an alias; the owner's mark said new concept.

## 2026-10-22 · Explorer: concept-count funnel in the right-hand panel (owner request, $0)
- **Decision:** a collapsible "How the concept count shrinks" funnel is pinned above the details in `#detail` (P&D: both `slice3_c2` and `slice3_c3`, whole book or the section chosen in the Pipeline tab; SciNLP: per paper). Steps: candidates offered (proposed nodes + candidates the checks rejected as *new concepts*) -> after checks (proposed nodes) -> after filter -> after merging duplicate names (distinct concepts the nodes map to) -> in the final graph -> with at least one relation. Each bar shows count, drop and a tooltip; a step with no recorded cause shows an explicit "Unaccounted difference" row. Reference implementation `explain/funnel.py`, browser mirror `conceptFunnel` in `kg_explorer_trace.js`, parity-tested for the whole book and every section.
- **Numbers (slice3_c2):** 1,393 -> 1,275 (-118 rejected) -> 1,275 (0 pruned recorded) -> 1,243 (-32 merged) -> 1,243 -> 598 with a relation. `slice3_c3`: 1,523 -> 1,401 -> 1,401 -> 1,352 (3 nodes have no recorded mapping, each counted as its own concept) -> 1,351 (**1 unaccounted**, shown) -> 600.
- **Reason / limits:** rejected *existing-mention* candidates (155 in `slice3_c2`) are mentions, not concepts, so they are excluded. The pruner records no pruned concept in the retained runs. SciNLP has no per-section split and its funnel comes from `v4_concepts.json` and the checkpoint; no estimate is made where a file is absent ("Not recorded for this run"). Gold-free.
- **Alternatives:** counting rejected mentions in the first bar (rejected: inflates the funnel with non-concepts); a separate tab (rejected: the owner asked for the right side).

Overlaps merged (subclassOf->is_a, partOf->part_of, UsedFor->has_purpose, compareWith->contrasts_with, trainedWith->uses); six relations added (subtask_of, enhanced_by, measured_by, evaluated_by, evaluated_on, similar_to) plus the family `evaluation`. New optional schema field `RelationType.external_labels`; `apply_patch` gained `adds.families` and `adds.external_labels`. Hierarchy set is derived from the registry ({is_a, subtask_of} = SciNLP {subclassOf, subtaskOf}). The CR-022 mapping draft is removed (the registry carries the mapping).
- **Reason:** owner wants one vocabulary. Rule 11 is followed (new registry version plus this entry; nothing added in code or prompts).
- **Caveats:** the definitions, examples and near-misses of the six new relations are my drafts and need owner review; `similar_to` is worded so it never creates or implies a merge (rule 21); the `uses` <- `trainedWith` and `part_of` <- `partOf` merges are close, not exact; SciNLP-only relation types (task/method/dataset/metric) are covered by domain `Any`. Supersedes the 2026-10-10 Research ruling "never merged with v1.3". Production default stays v1.3 until the owner adopts v1.4 (relation prompts must be re-versioned and the graph re-run).
- **Alternatives:** folding subtaskOf into part_of[phase] or is_a (rejected: loses the task hierarchy); folding similarWith into contrasts_with (rejected: opposite meaning); switching the production default now (rejected: changes every new run).

## 2026-10-22 · CR-025: three-model comparison on SciNLP dev (owner request)
- **Decision:** compared gpt-6-luna, o4-mini and gpt-6-sol (each on both tiers) on the 6 SciNLP dev papers, E0, new run ids; test split untouched. o4-mini needed two deviations (effort low, output caps x3) because it truncated at the production settings. Overlay profiles may now also set `llm.escalation` and `llm.max_output_tokens`.
- **Result (IDXR1-P1, pooled):** relation F1 luna 0.114 ($0.18), o4-mini 0.081 ($1.98), sol 0.154 ($3.63); concept F1 0.420 / 0.369 / 0.502; reference mixed-tier runs 0.109 and 0.096 (spread about 0.013). Report `reports/cr025/comparison.md`.
- **Reading:** price does not rank quality here: luna is about 20x cheaper than sol for about 0.04 lower relation F1 (about 3x the reference spread, one run, 6 papers); o4-mini is dearer than luna and scored lowest, but only under the deviating settings. No default changed.
- **Alternatives:** o4-mini at medium effort with raised caps only (still truncated); a different third model.

## 2026-10-23 · CR-032: broader Hierarchy F1 `HIER-T1` / `HIER-T1-SURF-R1` (scorer only)
- **Decision:** new variants beside the frozen CR-017 / CR-022 numbers: closure per hierarchy relation (never mixing relations; `part_of` per part type; `part_of` without a part type direct-only), pairs pooled and keyed (relation, concept, ancestor). The hierarchy set is derived from the registry (family `classification_structure`, `transitive` true or `within_same_part_type`) with no registry change (rule 11); config `configs/hierarchy_eval.yaml`; module `src/cumap/cr032/` (CR-026 is taken). SciNLP: `HIER-T1-SURF-R1` (R1 key + acronym <-> long form in one paper; unambiguous short forms only) as a labelled cross-check; a blind scoped identity sheet (hierarchy-relation endpoints, 90 rows) for the real-identity figure, which waits for the owner's marks.

- **Alternatives:** all directed edges (rejected); an explicit `hierarchy: true` registry flag (needs a new registry version; deferred); hard-coding the set (rejected, rule 11).

## 2026-10-23 · CR-033 PRE-REGISTRATION: SciNLP concept-level fact F1 (`FACT-C1`, `FACT-C1-SURF-R1`) — written before any test-split figure is computed (rule 9)
- **Definition (`FACT-C1`):** gold facts = SciNLP's mention-level gold relations collapsed to unique (source concept, relation, target concept) facts; a relation marked in several sentences is one fact; symmetric relations (from the registry) are unordered. Predicted facts = the placed predicted relations of the graph, one per (source concept, relation, target concept), each mapped to gold concepts through the same identity step. Score = P / R / F1 over the two fact sets; each fact counts once on each side. A predicted concept that maps to no gold concept keeps a synthetic id, so its facts can only be false positives; a gold concept with no predicted node loses its facts. Predicted edges that cannot be placed are false positives in every variant but `FROZEN` (rule 35).
- **Identity rule (the only version of this score, owner 2026-10-23):** `FACT-C1-SURF-R1` = R1-key pseudo-nodes (CR-008 R1 name key) plus acronym <-> long form pairs within one paper (unambiguous short forms only), identical to `HIER-T1-SURF-R1` (CR-032). It infers identity from strings and is always labelled so; no full identity sheet is planned.
- **Evidence:** not used for matching. Reported beside it: the share of true-positive facts whose own quote also matches a gold mention-level relation (the `IDX-P1` strict view), so "right fact, different sentence" is a number.
- **Status of the figure:** `IDX-P1` stays the headline and `IDX-P3` stays the upper bound; `FACT-C1-SURF-R1` is a labelled companion score, not a headline candidate, because its identity is inferred from strings. Any later headline change is a logged owner decision, never chosen on which number is higher.
- **Grounding variants reported:** `FROZEN` and `IDX-P1` (plus `IDXR1-P1` for the CR-021 rebuild), each beside the frozen CR-012 / CR-019 number.
- **Validation order:** fixtures, then SciNLP dev numbers, then (after this entry is committed) the test split once: frozen E0 run, CR-021 rebuild, E1 / E3-M / E4-M oracle runs. Determinism gate: the frozen CR-012 and CR-019 SciNLP figures reproduce before any new figure.
- **Alternatives rejected:** switching the headline to `IDX-P3` (credits edges from sentences the classifier never read, can count one edge as several true positives, and would be post-hoc); a full identity annotation of the 590 endpoint mentions (owner declined).

## 2026-10-23 · CR-034: qualifier code checks (part A of the qualifier amendments), switch default off
- **Decision:** `qualifier_checks: "off" | "v1"` (`configs/relation_policy.yaml`, default off; argument on `relations_stage` / `classify_pair_v3`). `v1`: negated edge without a negation cue is rejected with reason `negation_without_cue` and logged (owner default D-4); a misfit qualifier (registry `applies_to`) and a surface phrase not shorter than the quote are recorded as flags only. `V3Result.qualifier_flags` is serialised only when non-empty, so older results and the default are byte-identical. Readers tolerate a missing `dimension` / `part_type` and a narrower modality set (change 7). A new run records the mode in `selection_stats` only when it is not `off`.
- **Reason:** handoff §10 changes 5 and 7: 1 of 3 negated edges in `slice3_c2` is wrong ("can only be built using fixed-length cells"), `part_type` sits on 2 edges whose relation does not take it, `dimension` on 7 non-comparisons, and 128 of 549 surface phrases are as long as the quote.
- **Alternatives:** reject misfit qualifiers and long surface phrases (rejected: would remove up to 24% of edges produced by the old prompt versions; the prompt amendment, CR-038, is the real fix); keep the negation edge as affirmed and flag it (the owner chose rejection, D-4).
- **Not done:** prompts v6, registry v1.5, removal of `trades_off_with` from `DIMENSION_RELATIONS` (CR-038); no paid measurement.

## 2026-10-23 · CR-035: identity resolver, deterministic part (U0-U4) and the registry search, switch `identity_resolver: legacy | u1` (default legacy, not adopted)
- **Decision:** `src/cumap/expert_kg/identity.py` (U0 lexicon `different`, U1 lexicon `same`, U2 same name form, U3 acronym <-> long form, U4-strong alias statement, U4-weak review) over the existing `AliasContext.pair_rule`; `configs/identity_rules.yaml` (order, legacy-id map, search recipe, thresholds 0.6 / 0.70, `not_a_concept` per input with D-5: B and C review flag, node kept); `configs/concept_policy.yaml: identity_resolver: legacy`. Under `u1`: input A gets the U0 veto and U4 statements (`runner._apply`; U0 skips the adjudicator); input B searches with `ConceptRegistry.top_k_similar_forms` ("form. gloss", every form, best form logged, a merge's new alias is embedded on next use); the merge log keeps each entry's own rule id with `legacy_rule_id` (fixes the `G-link` relabelling of rejected links); weak cues become review rows; a pair U0-U4 cannot decide falls back to the existing LLM path (adjudicator / `canonicalize` v2), so no new prompt is needed. The value is recorded in `v4_concepts.json` extra when not `legacy`.

Search on the 45 evaluable owner-marked `same` pairs (CR-007 / CR-008 sheets; 8 have no concept in the run): candidate recall legacy 24 / u1 37 (top 5, similarity >= 0.6, not tuned); mentions reaching the LLM judge (approximation, final registry): legacy 646 / u1 503 of 1,243.
- **PRE-REGISTERED MEASUREMENT BEFORE ADOPTION (proposed; waits for the owner's review of the U5 draft and a choice of held-out data):** development = CR-021 dev split (72 rows) for input A and the owner-marked CR-005 / CR-007 / CR-008 merge-sheet pairs for input B, excluding pairs the lexicon already covers; held-out = a second look at the CR-021 held-out split (labelled as such) or a fresh owner-marked sheet of about 100 B-path pairs from `slice3_c2`; adopt only if U5 `same` precision is not lower than the adjudicator v2 held-out value by more than 0.03 and the U0-U4 decisions on a $0 replay equal today's except the intended changes; cap about $3 following rule 7. Nothing was run.

## 2026-10-23 · CR-036: fold "verify and assemble" into classification; "save graph" export (`assembly: legacy | v2`, default legacy, not adopted)
- **Decision:** switch `assembly` in `configs/relation_policy.yaml` (quoted). `v2`: registry version and hash recorded in the run and checked on load (a run with no record is refused); `classify_pair_v3(direction_by_types=True)` stores the type-valid orientation instead of rejecting a domain/range error (flag `direction_by_types` on the result, logged); `edge_store.build_edge_store` stores directed edges keyed (source, relation, target) with the pair id kept, normalises symmetric relations, merges identical edges with all quotes, keeps several relations per pair, flags `conflicts_with`, and puts non-selected (sample) results in a separate list; `slice_rerun.save_graph_stage` writes the same snapshot files without cycle / domain-range checks (plus `edges_detail.jsonl`, `edge_store.json`, manifest `assembly` / `registry`); the misconception stage reuses an existing result (`find_existing_result`) instead of classifying the same proposed edge again. Legacy code paths are unchanged (`build_snapshots_stage` gained keyword-only options whose defaults are the old behaviour); `RelationRegistry.from_yaml` now sets `source_path`; `report/data.py` tolerates `pair~relation` edge ids. The edge store is built at export from the classification results (equivalent to writing it as results arrive, and it needs no checkpoint schema change).
- **Reason:** owner decision 2026-10-22 (handoff §12). Every check the old stage did either never fired on `slice3_c2` (cycles 0, domain/range 0, retired relations 0) or repeats one that classification already does.
- **Alternatives:** keep the stage (rejected by the owner); change the checkpoint schema to hold the store (rejected: byte-identical replays); reject a type-invalid orientation as before (the owner chose to set the valid one; the model's own statement describes the orientation it chose, so recovered edges need the owner's review beside the domain/range review).
- **Replay on `slice3_c2` (`reports/cr036/assembly_replay_slice3_c2.md`):** 529 selected edge results, legacy 528 snapshot edges, v2 store 528 (6 differ only by sorted symmetric ids), 1 identical edge merged, 0 conflicts, 0 directions changed in stored edges, 50 measurement results kept out, and 14 of the 58 classification-time domain/range rejections have a type-valid reverse orientation (what `v2` would store). `slice3_c2` records no registry version, so `v2` refuses it; it stays `legacy`.
- **Not done:** the online bookkeeping (`finish_concepts` is still called at the end of the concept stage), per-occurrence classification, the presentation's stage 4 box, a paid run.

- **Decision:** new files only. `prompts/identity_judge/v1.md` (built from `rejected_link_adjudicator` v2 with `narrower` / `broader` added; static instructions and examples first, the case last; closed answer set `same / narrower / broader / new / not_a_concept`; an exact-substring evidence quote), `src/cumap/expert_kg/identity_judge.py` (the schema is built from `configs/identity_rules.yaml` `judge.options`, every field required, no defaults; one strong-tier call through `llm/client.py`; a failed quote becomes `no_decision`), the `judge` section of `configs/identity_rules.yaml`, `identity_judge: "off"` in `configs/concept_policy.yaml`. - **Decision:** new files only (rules 8, 11, 12). Registry `configs/relations_v1.6.yaml` (patch `CR-039-relations-v1.6.yaml`) = **v1.5 + a `misconception` family** whose one relation `conflated_with` has layer `misconception` (never semantic, rule 22); built on v1.5 (a CR-038 draft of v1.3), not v1.4. Prompts `relation_family/v5.md` (when to choose the family: a belief presented as wrong; a plain negation stays a negated relation) and `relation_choice/v7.md` (v6 rules plus the misconception rules), built on the production v3 family text and the CR-038 v6 choice draft. `src/cumap/expert_kg/relations_v7.py`: the closed-choice schema `build_misconception_choice` (wrong statement = registry relation or `conflated_with`, direction, polarity; perturbation type; correct statement or `none`; two verbatim quotes; every field required, no defaults) and `build_relation_choice_v7` (v6 schema for ordinary families). `classify_pair_v3(misconception_source="classification")` returns outcome `misconception` (quotes verified as exact substrings of the sentence) or `no_relation` / `not_a_misconception`; without the switch a v1.6 `misconception` family answer is `other` / `family_misconception_not_enabled`. `misconception.layer_from_classification` builds the layer with the legacy code checks (`check_item`, `_check_proposed`, `consistent`): the item links to the expert edge or lexicon `different` entry it contradicts; a correct edge not in the graph is classified by a verifier or waits as `needs_correct_edge`; every failure goes to `needs_review`; the output has the legacy layer's shape (explorer and readers unchanged). The $0 cue scan is kept as a selection-priority signal only (`cue_pair_priority`, `select_pairs(priority_pairs=)`, phase 0c, default unchanged). `relations_stage` needs registry v1.6 and `qualifier_amendments: "v6"` and defaults to prompts `("v5", "v7", "v6")`; `kg misconceptions` builds the layer from classification results at $0 when the switch is on. The legacy stage is untouched and stays the default.
- **Reason:** owner decision 2026-10-22 (handoff §14): one pipeline instead of two, misconceptions found by the same calls as relations.
- **Alternatives:** keep the separate structuring stage (kept as `legacy` until the measurement passes); make `conflated_with` a registry relation of an existing family (rejected: rule 22 keeps it out of the semantic layer).
- **Not done / stop:** the §14 measurement (P&D development sections and `slice3_c2`, new run ids, recall of today's 4 items and the owner-confirmed warnings, false-positive rate on the 75 "not a warning" cue sentences, unchanged relation results on non-misconception pairs) waits for the owner's review of the drafts; the two-sentence window for misconception-family pairs and the Option C family step on every candidate are not built.

## 2026-10-24 · CR-043 pre-registration: the "all pairs" arm (written before the arm is run)
- **Arm:** `cumap.cr043.arm_allpairs` (tag `cr043allp`): the baseline's finished concept stage is copied, and every candidate pair of a paper is classified (pair budget = the whole same-sentence pool) instead of the D3 budget. ## 2026-10-24 · CR-043 result: the "all pairs" arm is not adopted (pre-registered rule)

Pair recall 0.680 -> 0.829, macro F1 0.302 -> 0.341, fact F1 0.133 -> 0.142; P0 losses 71 -> 38; 1846 pairs classified (all of the pool), 519 edges. Cost $0.158 (luna).
- **Decision:** keep the D3 pair budget for SciNLP runs. The arm's runs stay as a comparison (KG explorer Run option "CR-043 all-pairs arm (not adopted)").
- **Reason:** the rule was written before the run; classifying every pair raised recall but added about as many unmarked edges, so pair delivery is not the main bottleneck. The next target is concept spans (N0 + N1 = 55% of losses).
- **Alternatives:** adopting on the recall gain (refused: no post-hoc change of the rule).

## 2026-10-24 · CR-048 result: the misconception family fails its pre-registered rule; `misconception_source` stays `legacy`

Status `KEEP_LEGACY`.
- **Beside it:** the classification flagged 3 of the 10 needs-correct-edge sentences and 8 pairs in all (perturbations: wrong relation 4, conflation 2, polarity flip 1, condition error 1); the code-checked layer kept 0 items (6 need review, 2 need a correct edge, 1 not a warning).
- **Why (diagnosis, $0):** (1) coverage: 2 of the 4 item sentences hold no classifiable pair (the wrong belief and the concepts are in different sentences, e.g. "bit rate isn't necessarily less than or equal to the baud rate" names no concept pair of the item; the forwarding / routing table sentence matches no pair); (2) on the 2 classifiable item sentences the family step never chose `misconception` (it chose dependency, mechanism and classification families); (3) the new family prompt (v5) also moved ordinary pairs: 23 of the 94 P&D pairs changed answer, about half with a changed family.
- **Decision:** keep the legacy misconception stage (cue scan + structuring call). The drafts stay in the repository, off. Refinement the owner flagged as likely: a two-sentence window for cue pairs, a family prompt that leaves ordinary families unchanged (measured on the P&D pairs first), and examples of textbook warnings; any refinement is a new prompt version and a new pre-registered measurement.

## 2026-10-24 · CR-049 pre-registration: identity judge v2 with GraphJudge elements (owner: bring in elements of the paper; reuse the CR-021 held-out split) — written before any call
- **What is taken from GraphJudge** (Huang et al., EMNLP 2025): (1) *entity-centric context*: the judge sees a short context pack for each side, up to 3 sentences that use the TERM (its own sentence first) and up to 3 other sentences that use the CONCEPT (same section first, then the rest of the text; whole-word match on the name and aliases; `identity_judge.context_pack`, context only, never a mention, rule 24); (2) *graph judgement*: one proposed claim ("TERM is the same concept as CONCEPT") verified against those packs, false unless supported. **Not taken:** fine-tuning an open model as the judge (a few hundred marked pairs, new infrastructure, and rule 4); the paper's own result that untuned LLM judges are weak (about 0.6 on SciERC) is why this is measured. Negative sampling becomes a reported stress set (approved lexicon `different` pairs), not training data.
- **New files:** `prompts/identity_judge/v2.md` (v1 untouched), `identity_judge.context_pack` / `render_pack`, `load_judge_prompt(version)`, `judge_pair(term_context=, concept_context=)` (v1 rendering unchanged); `cumap.cr049.measure`, `cumap.cr049.score`. The production switch still accepts only `off` / `v1`: the production call sites pass no packs yet.
- **Rows:** the CR-021 sheet (owner-checked, model-assisted labels) with its fixed split; SciNLP test rows left out of every arm (rule 9): dev 69, held-out 65. **Held-out is a second look** (owner, 2026-10-24). Arms on the same rows and the same profile (luna): `ADJ` = rejected_link_adjudicator v2 (production), `J1` = identity_judge v1, `J2` = identity_judge v2. Judge answers map: same -> same_as_target; narrower / broader / new -> new_concept.
- **Rule (held-out):** J2 passes if its `same` precision is at least ADJ's minus **0.03**, its `new_concept` and `same` precisions each have a Wilson 95% lower bound **>= 0.70**, and at most **20%** of gold same rows are decided new_concept (the CR-021 rules). Passing makes J2 the judge for the u1 identity path; switching `identity_resolver: u1` on, and wiring the packs into the production call sites, stay owner decisions.
- **Order and cost:** dev (all arms) first, then held-out once, then the stress set; dry run and `--limit 20` first; cap $3 (estimate $0.2).

## 2026-10-24 · CR-049 result: identity judge v2 fails its pre-registered rule; finding about the adjudicator on the luna profile
- **Result** (`reports/cr049/identity_judge.md`, luna, $0.094 of $3; held-out = second look, 65 rows): J2 (v2, context packs) accuracy 0.769; `same` precision 10/14 = 0.714 (ADJ 0.739: within 0.03, met), Wilson LB **0.454** (rule 0.70, not met); `new_concept` 38/46 = 0.826, LB **0.693** (not met); gold same rows decided new **8/20 = 0.40** (rule at most 0.20, not met). Status `KEEP_ADJ`. Dev showed the same pattern before held-out was run (J2 `same` 12/13 but 12 of 24 gold same rows split).
- **Why:** the context packs and the "false unless supported" framing make the judge too cautious: one sentence where a pair is used a little differently is enough for it to answer narrower / new, so many true variants are split (the CR-021 sheet counts inflection and contextual variants as same).
- **Beside it (held-out, same rows, same profile):** J1 (v1, one sentence) accuracy **0.908**, `same` 18/21 = 0.857 (LB 0.654), `new_concept` 39/41 = 0.951 (LB 0.839), same rows decided new 2/20; ADJ (rejected_link_adjudicator v2, production) accuracy 0.846, `same` 17/23 = 0.739 (LB **0.535**), `new_concept` 37/40 = 0.925 (LB 0.801), 3/20. Stress set (25 approved lexicon `different` pairs): ADJ 7, J1 7, J2 6 decided same; in production the lexicon veto (U0 / R0) decides these pairs before any judge is called.
- **Finding:** CR-021 adopted the adjudicator on held-out `same` precision 0.952 (LB 0.773) with the then strong tier (gpt-6-sol). Under the current luna profile (CR-026) the same prompt on the same rows gives 0.739 (LB 0.535): the aliasing half of the adjudicator would not pass its own adoption rule on luna. Promotion (`new_concept`, LB 0.801) still passes. Options for the owner: run the identity judge on sol (an `escalate`-style tier for this task only; about 124 calls per P&D rebuild), restrict the luna adjudicator to promotion, or measure J1 as the replacement in a new CR. No switch is changed here.
- **Decision:** v2 is not adopted; `identity_judge` stays `off`, the production adjudicator is unchanged. A v3 (calibrated packs) would be a new prompt version with a fresh pre-registration; the held-out split has now been looked at twice.

## 2026-10-24 · CR-050 opened (owner: option 3): measure identity judge v1 as the replacement for the adjudicator on the luna profile, on a FRESH owner sheet — pre-registration (written before any call and before the sheet is marked)
- **Why fresh:** the CR-021 held-out split has been looked at twice (CR-021 adoption, CR-049), and J1's numbers on it are known; a third look would not be a fair adoption test.
- **Sheet:** `cumap.cr050.sheet` -> `data/interim/suggestions/cr050_identity_sheet_blind.csv` (local, gitignored: holds CC BY-NC SciNLP text): 103 `G-link-rejected` links from runs made after the CR-021 sheet (P&D rebuild `slice3_c3`: 84; CR-043 SciNLP train / dev papers: 19), minus every item already on the CR-021 sheet and minus empty or dash-only surfaces; rows in a fixed hash order; blind (no model decision, reason or rule output). Same columns and marking as CR-021 (distinct yes/no, same-as-target yes/no). The owner marks it and saves it under `data/gold/` (rule 2). No SciNLP test paper is used.
- **Arms (after marking), same rows, luna profile:** `ADJ` = rejected_link_adjudicator v2 (production), `J1` = identity_judge v1 (one sentence + description; same -> same_as_target, narrower / broader / new -> new_concept). Each row is judged once; the whole sheet is the test set (no tuning on it).
- **Rule:** J1 replaces the adjudicator for rejected links on luna if, on the marked sheet, J1's `same` precision and `new_concept` precision each have a Wilson 95% lower bound **>= 0.70**, at most **20%** of gold same rows are decided new_concept, and J1's accuracy is **not lower** than ADJ's on the same rows. Otherwise the adjudicator stays (and the luna aliasing gap from CR-049 stays open for the owner).
- **Cost:** about 206 luna calls (estimate $0.08); cap $1; dry run and `--limit 20` first.

## 2026-10-24 · CR-052: why concept endpoints are missed (N0 / N1) in the CR-043 baseline ($0 analysis)
- **What:** `cumap.cr052.spans` classifies every distinct gold endpoint mention of a relation lost at N0 / N1 (`IDXR1-P1`, 20 papers): 263 endpoints (136 N1, 127 N0). Report `reports/cr052/span_analysis.md`.
- **N1 (span differs, 136):** the prediction is mostly SHORTER than the gold (77), split across several mentions (32) or longer (27). Token difference: missing modifier or head (63; 43 of them are gold = prediction + a trailing word, mostly a generic head: approach 14, model 9, dataset 3, system / method / algorithm 2 each; 17 are a dropped leading modifier such as "standard fine-tuning", "binary classification"), split (32; 19 of them are the gold convention of including a following "( ACRONYM )", e.g. "word sense disambiguation ( WSD )"), extra modifier (24), parenthetical / acronym (6), prepositional tail (5), coordination (3), punctuation (3). The predicted type agrees with the gold type in 125 of 136.
- **N0 (no overlap, 127):** every one has no node with that surface (no case of an unplaced occurrence). Gold types: method 71, metric 27, task 26, dataset 3; 58 are one token: generic metrics the generator does not extract ("accuracy", "entropy", "fluency", "coverage"), short acronyms or variant names ("MLM", "LMs", "HPSG", "Auto L" / "Auto T"), and some multi-word variants ("smaller language models", "phrase-based model ( PBM )").
- **$0 rescoring variant `IDXR1X-P1`** (`cumap.cr052.extend`; two gold-free extension rules, absorb a following "( ACRONYM )" and a trailing generic head noun; reported beside `IDXR1-P1`, rule 35): `cr043base` strict relation F1 0.107 -> **0.125**, concept F1 0.419 -> 0.443; current settings (`cr047amend`) 0.114 -> **0.129**. The rules were written after reading these 20 papers' errors, so the gain is optimistic.
- **Not changed:** no pipeline or scorer change. Next (owner decision): pre-register the two rules as a placement change for new SciNLP runs and confirm them on the other 37 train papers (not yet run; about $1.5 with the current settings); the N0 recall gap (metrics, acronyms) needs a generator-prompt change, measured separately.

Reported beside: dev / train split (the v6 lists come from the train gold, so train numbers are optimistic; dev is not), recall / precision, CR-054 loss stages, M6 hints added / rejected, `IDXR1X-P1`.

- **Cost:** 2 papers first, then all; cap $2 (estimate about $0.7 for A, $1.0 for B).

## 2026-10-25 · CR-055 result: the concept-recall bundle raises recall but fails its pre-registered rule; profile v3 stays
- **Result** (20 papers, `IDXR1-P1`; reports `reports/cr043/cr055a.md`, `cr055b.md`; spend A $0.538, B $0.674): concept F1 control `cr053v5` **0.485** (P 0.371, R 0.701) -> A **0.491** (P 0.370, R 0.731; +0.006, rule >= 0.02) -> B **0.483** (P 0.356, R 0.751). Strict relation F1 0.136 -> A 0.125 -> B 0.122 (rule: does not fall; both fell). Status: neither arm adopted; new SciNLP runs keep profile v3 (prompt v5_scinlp). The switches stay in the code, off unless a profile turns them on.
- **What worked:** recall rose as intended: missed mentions 608 -> 546 (A) -> 505 (B); never proposed 269 -> 211 -> 164; lost after the generator 40 -> 26 -> 20 (carry-forward and quote repair). M6 hints were mostly rejected by the generator (2-paper check: 22 of 28).
- **Why it failed:** precision did not rise with recall (more concepts, 1046 -> 1100 -> 1269 nodes, many of them unmarked), and the extra concepts crowd the pair budget: candidate pairs 1393 -> 1544 -> 1840, pairs never classified (P0) 57 -> 75 -> 97, pair recall 0.753 -> 0.688 -> 0.624, so relation F1 fell although dev relation F1 rose (0.142 -> 0.159 -> 0.176) while train fell (0.133 -> 0.108 -> 0.095).
- **Beside it:** `IDXR1X-P1` (CR-052 span rules): control concept 0.513 / relation 0.163; A 0.520 / 0.146; B 0.509 / 0.139. Fact F1 0.166 -> 0.153 -> 0.150.
- **Reading:** concept recall can be bought, but on SciNLP it costs relation F1 through the fixed pair budget; a recall change needs the pair budget (or pair ranking) to scale with it, measured together.

## 2026-10-27 · CR-058: read-only SciNLP pipeline diagnostics page (stage funnel, oracle ladder, gold vs built graphs)
- **Decision:** `src/cumap/cr058/` (`funnel.py`, `graphs.py`, `page.py`, template `page.html`) builds one self-contained offline page, `reports/cr058/pipeline.html` (`uv run python -m cumap.cr058.page`, $0, no model call). It is gitignored because it embeds SciNLP-derived text (CC BY-NC); `reports/cr058/summary.{json,md}` hold numbers only and are committed. Two panels, never pooled: development = the 57 train + dev papers with profile v5 (`cr055ballp` first 20, `cr057rest` other 37); held-out = the 3 test papers, frozen CR-012 oracle runs E0 / E1 / E3-M / E4-M (older pipeline), read for reporting only (rule 9, as CR-031). Owner choices (2026-10-26): both panels labelled, standalone page, built after CR-057 merged.
- **Definitions (grounding `IDXR1-P1` everywhere, rule 35):** concept P / R / F1 after each concept component = the concepts existing after it (generator first pass, verifier on it, corrective round, node store after the pruner, consolidation) placed with the IDXR1-P1 rule and scored mention level by the frozen entity-strict scorer (SciNLP has no identity gold); an existing mention counts under the card node it names. Verifier drops: an item is "correct" when one of its forms has the R1 key of a gold mention surface of the paper, else "wrong" (not a gold concept; SciNLP gold is sparse). A classified pair is correct when a gold relation links placed mentions of its two concepts; family right = SciNLP family on a correct pair; choice right = the gold relation and direction; code-check drops and assembly drops are split by whether the dropped pair had the right relation. Pair recall at a step = gold relations reaching it / gold relations with both endpoints placed. Consolidation counts canonicalisation merges and generator-link decisions separately; the checkpoint's relabelled `G-link` copies of generator-stage entries are skipped (not counted twice). Gold graph nodes = gold mentions grouped by the CR-032 R1 + acronym rule (identity inferred from strings, labelled); both graphs share one layout.
- **Gates:** the pooled final-stage numbers equal the CR-043 scorecards (concept, relation, hierarchy H2, fact F1, macro F1; both sets) and the CR-031 ladder (`IDXR1-P1`, all four levels): all 16 pass; the source checkpoints and concept files hash-identical before and after.
- **Reading (development, 57 papers):** of 1,354 gold relations, 673 have both endpoints placed (-681: concept spans and missing concepts are the largest loss), 590 classified (pair recall 87.7%), 484 family right, 270 choice right (-214: the second-largest loss), 249 pass the checks, 248 assembled, 180 credited (-68 right fact on another sentence). Concept F1 0.475 after the generator's first pass, 0.466 after consolidation (the corrective round loses on the 37 other papers, 0.473 -> 0.459). The verifier drops 138 non-gold and 29 gold new-concept items; the code checks drop 968 wrong and 21 right pairs (domain_range 849 / 14).
- **Alternatives:** a tab in the KG explorer (touches the explorer; owner chose standalone); computing the funnel on the test split as well (kept to the oracle ladder plus the E0 record, since the test runs use the older pipeline).

## 2026-10-27 · CR-059 DRAFT: a domain-general, type-free concept generator with a gold-free domain profile; endpoint check by the choice step (SciNLP only) — pre-registered before any call
- **Owner decisions (2026-10-27):** remove node types from the generator and its prompt; replace the node-type domain/range check by the relation choice step judging whether the endpoints fit the chosen relation (option b); SciNLP only in this CR (P&D would need an untyped strict E2E variant); the domain profile is generated assuming no gold.
- **Built (drafts, mock-fixture tests only, never run):** `prompts/concept_generator/v7.md` (type-free core; every domain-specific line comes from profile slots), schema `ConceptGeneratorUntypedLLM` (`concepts_v4/schema.py`: an empty type list gives schemas with no `node_type`; the runner gives every item the generic internal type `Concept`, which every downstream rule already treats as untyped; cards drop the type column); `src/cumap/cr059/domain_profile.py` (a fixed, domain-independent checklist of ten kinds of concept, the profile format, an approved-only loader, deterministic slot rendering); `src/cumap/cr059/draft.py` + `prompts/domain_profile/v1.md` (one strong-tier call drafts a profile from 2 excerpts of each of 3 SciNLP train papers of the first set and the owner's purpose sentence; no gold; every term carries a quote checked as an exact substring, failures dropped and logged; output `data/interim/suggestions/domain_profile_scinlp_draft.yaml`, status draft; the owner approves by writing `configs/domain_profiles/scinlp_v1.yaml` with status approved, code never approves); `prompts/relation_choice/v8.md` + `expert_kg/relations_v8.py` (v6 + `endpoint_fit` fits / does_not_fit and a reason; a misfit is rejected with reason `endpoint_fit`; in this mode node types are never read); switch `endpoint_check: types | choice` read from the benchmark profile (default `types`, so every earlier run and the P&D path are unchanged); profiles `configs/scinlp_profile_v7.yaml` (v5 + generator v7 + domain profile + endpoint_check choice) and `v7r` (v5 + endpoint_check choice only); runner `cumap.cr059.scinlp_run` (refuses to start until the domain profile is approved) and the relations-only arm `cumap.cr059.arm_relations`. `configs/default.yaml` is hash-pinned (CR-011 handoff), so the drafter sets its own output limit at run time.
- **Pre-registered measurement (SciNLP train + dev only, grounding `IDXR1-P1`, frozen scorers, CR-058 funnel for every arm):** baseline `cr055ballp` (profile v5, first 20 papers). *Arm R* `cr059relR`: the v5 concepts and pair selection copied, relations re-run under v7r. Adopt choice b if strict relation F1 >= baseline - 0.005; report the endpoint_fit drops split into correct / wrong pairs beside the domain_range drops of the baseline. *Arm F* `cr059v7`: profile v7 end to end on the first 20. Adopt profile v7 for new SciNLP runs only if concept recall >= baseline - 0.01 AND strict relation F1 >= baseline - 0.005 on the first 20, AND the same two conditions hold on the other 37 train papers against `cr057rest` (a second look at those papers, labelled as such). The rule is non-inferiority because the aim is a domain-general, gold-free prompt, not a gain; typed metrics are reported as not applicable. Order: drafter (owner approves the profile first), arm R, arm F first 20, arm F other 37; 2 papers first each time.
- **Cost:** drafter under $0.01; arm R about $0.2; arm F about $0.8 (first 20) + $1.9 (other 37); cap $4 for CR-059.
- **Known risk:** the gold-free profile describes the documents and the purpose, not SciNLP's annotation convention (which marks named instances only), so concept precision and relation F1 may fall against v5, whose word lists came from train-paper marking rates; the measurement shows the cost of generality.
- **Alternatives:** keep node types in the generator and drop only the domain/range check (owner: remove types); calibrate the profile from train-split gold (owner: assume no gold); include P&D now (owner: SciNLP first).
- **2026-10-27 · CR-059 cap amended (owner, before any arm F result):** the approved gold-free profile roughly doubles the concepts (101 -> 198 on 2021.acl-long.295) and multiplies the pairs (176 -> 616), so arm F on both sets would cost about $8-9. Owner: run arm F on the first 20 papers only for now; CR-059 cap $4 -> $5. The adoption rule is unchanged (it still needs both sets); the other 37 papers run only if the first 20 pass, after asking the owner. The relation stage's sol-priced per-paper preflight bound is raised to $6 for this arm through `cumap.cr043.run --max-usd-per-doc` (real luna spend stays capped by `--cap-usd`).

## 2026-10-28 · CR-059 results so far: choice b and profile v1 not adopted (arm F stopped after 2 papers, owner)
- **Arm R** (`cr059relR`, the 20 first-set papers, v5 concepts and pairs, relations under v7r, $0.463): strict relation F1 (`IDXR1-P1`) 0.153 -> **0.139** (P 0.157 -> 0.125, R 0.149 -> 0.157; edges 459 -> 599): fails the rule (<= -0.005). The checks drop: v5 domain_range 2 correct / 328 wrong pairs; v7r endpoint_fit 0 correct / 95 wrong. The choice-step judgement is precise but catches 29% of what the node-type check caught (+6 correct, +134 wrong edges). Choice b is not adopted; `endpoint_check` stays `types` by default.
- **Arm F** (`cr059v7`, profile v7 with the approved gold-free profile v1): stopped by its $0.50 limit after 2 papers ($0.521, $0.26 per paper, 2x the estimate); owner chose to stop rather than raise the cap to about $6. On those 2 dev papers against v5: concepts 182 -> 462, pairs 274 -> 1,274, concept P / R 0.559 / 0.792 -> 0.238 / 0.795 (+1 gold mention of 375), strict relation F1 0.249 -> 0.121. Reported as a PARTIAL arm (2 of the 20 pre-registered papers, both dev); the effect is large and the owner recorded profile v1 as not adopted.
- **Reading:** removing node types costs the domain/range check, which was a strong precision filter; profile v1's broad reading (specificity field_terms, all ten categories, most "named or general") adds non-gold concepts and almost no gold ones on SciNLP.
- **Spend:** CR-059 $0.99 of the $5 cap.
- **Next (owner):** a narrower profile v2 (owner edits of v1, not re-drafted, not gold-derived), measured the same way.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-060 pre-registration: span identity (A) and noun-phrase candidates (B), first 20 papers only — written before any computation
- **Owner (2026-10-30):** plan approved as written; measured on the 20 first-set papers only (6 dev + 14 train; no unseen check, so every gain is optimistic: the CR-052 head list was written after reading these papers). Control: profile v5 `cr055ballp`. Objective `IDXR1-P1` (frozen scorer, no change); `IDXR1X-P1` / `IDXR1N-P1` reported beside (rule 35).
- **Part A: head-variant identity, an assembly-stage alias rule (code only, $0).** Rule `R-head` (deterministic, gold-free, `configs/span_identity.yaml`): for every node form F (name or alias) and every occurrence of F in the paper followed directly by a generic head H (the CR-052 `HEADS` list, unchanged), the node gains the alias "F H" (evidence: that text, an exact substring, rule 3); any other node whose name or alias has the R1 key of "F H" merges into F's node (rule and evidence recorded, rule 19; a lexicon `different` entry vetoes, rule 20); edges are remapped, duplicate edges and self-loops from a merge dropped. Rule `R-acr`: F followed by "( ACRONYM )" gains the alias "F ( ACRONYM )" (the CR-052 `X-acr` convention). Two variants: **A-name** = R-head only where F is name-like (an uppercase letter or a digit), **A-all** = R-head for every form + R-acr. Applied after relation classification, so replaying it on the saved v5 checkpoints equals running it (exact, $0). Adopt the variant with the higher strict relation F1 among those meeting the rule below; none if neither.
- **Part B: noun-phrase candidates as a verifier hint (`M8`).** Per paper: spaCy noun chunks, trimmed of determiners / pronouns / numbers at the edges, plus each suffix ending at the chunk head; candidates no item covers. Score = mean percentile rank of five features with **equal weights fixed now**: paper frequency, C-value termhood, PageRank centrality in the sentence co-occurrence graph of candidates, embedding similarity to the candidate's sentences (mean), embedding similarity to the paper (mean of unit embeddings). The top k per unit go to the generator as hints to accept or reject (the generator writes every mention, rule 24; embeddings only rank, rule 1). **k** chosen at $0 from {5, 10, 15, 20, 30}: the k with the highest F1 of hint keys against the R1 keys of gold mentions the v5 run never proposed (ties: smaller k); recorded before the arm. Profile `scinlp_profile_v9` = v5 + M8; arm `cr060np`, 2 papers first, stop at $1.5.
- **Rule (both parts, recall-first as in CR-057):** concept recall (`IDXR1-P1`) at least **+0.01** AND strict relation F1 at most **-0.005** against v5 `cr055ballp`. B is judged against v5 alone; if A is adopted, B is also reported with A applied to both arms.
- **Cap:** $3 for CR-060.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-060 Part A result: span identity A-all ADOPTED (profile v10)
- **Replay on v5 `cr055ballp`, 20 first-set papers, `IDXR1-P1`, $0** (`cumap.cr060.measure_a`, `reports/cr060/part_a.md`; exact, the rule runs after relation classification). Control reproduces v5: concept P / R / F1 0.356 / 0.751 / 0.483, relation 0.157 / 0.149 / **0.153**.
  - **A-name** (head alias for name-like forms only): 41 aliases, 0 merges; concept recall 0.771 (+0.020), relation F1 **0.165** (+0.012). Passes.
  - **A-all** (head alias for every form + "( ACRONYM )" alias): 96 head aliases, 92 acronym aliases, 11 merges, 3 edges dropped (duplicates / self-loops made by a merge); concept P / R / F1 0.371 / 0.768 / 0.500, relation 0.174 / 0.161 / **0.167** (+0.014). Passes.
  - Beside it (rule 35): `IDXR1X-P1` relation F1 control 0.173, A-all 0.167 (the alias rule captures most of the CR-052 view inside the objective itself; the view now adds little).
- **Decision (pre-registered tie-break: higher relation F1):** A-all adopted. `configs/scinlp_profile_v10.yaml` = v5 + `span_identity: A-all`; runner `cumap.cr060.scinlp_run` (applies the step after a paper's run, keeps `checkpoint_pre_span_identity.json` and `span_identity.json`); `cumap.cr043.run` default runner v5 -> v10. Existing runs are not rewritten.
- **Caveats:** measured on the same 20 papers the CR-052 head list was written from (no unseen check, owner); some A-all merges join a bare modifier with its head variant ("trigram" + "trigram models", "inference" + "inference algorithms"), which the rule treats as one concept.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-060 Part B sizing: the noun-phrase hint cannot meet the rule; the arm is not run
- **Sizing ($0, `cumap.cr060.measure_b`, `reports/cr060/part_b_sizing.md`):** ranked noun-phrase candidates (`cumap.cr060.np_candidates`, the five pre-registered features, equal weights) against the 107 R1 keys of gold mentions v5 never proposed on the 20 papers. k per unit 5 / 10 / 15 / 20 / 30: hints 156 / 323 / 482 / 647 / 994, never-proposed keys hit 9 / 12 / 13 / 16 / 18 (precision 0.058 -> 0.018). **Pre-registered k = 5** (best F1, 0.068).
- **Why it fails:** of the 164 never-proposed gold mentions, 45 are not noun-chunk candidates (PDF artefacts "Nystro ¨ m", adjectival "phrase-based", long task phrases), 37 are filtered as already covered (a containing or contained item exists: "confidence" / "confidence measures", "language model"), 82 are uncovered candidates but rank low (median rank 84 among a unit's uncovered candidates): frequency, termhood, centrality and similarity to the sentence / paper do not separate what gold marks from other noun phrases.
- **Ceiling:** even if the generator accepted and placed every gold hint, concept recall could rise by at most **0.0074** at k = 5 (0.019 at k = 30, at 994 hints, 2% gold, the CR-059 over-proposal regime); the rule needs +0.01. The arm (`cr060np`, profile v9) is therefore **not run** ($0 spent); M8 is not wired into the verifier. The ranker and the sizing stay as code for a later design.
- **CR-060 spend:** $0 of the $3 cap.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-060 Part B2 pre-registration (owner): noun phrases AS concepts, a $0 diagnostic — written before computing
- **Owner (2026-10-30):** instead of hints, use the ranked noun-phrase candidates to generate concepts and see whether recall improves.
- **Rule 24 conflict:** mentions are written only by the generator; string matching only produces hints. So this is measured as a **labelled replay diagnostic** (`NPC-k`), never a production run; adopting it would need an explicit owner change of rule 24 (and a DECISIONS entry) first.
- **Method (`cumap.cr060.measure_c`, $0):** on each v5 `cr055ballp` checkpoint of the 20 first-set papers, add the top k ranked candidates per unit (`cumap.cr060.np_candidates`, the pre-registered features, equal weights) that no v5 concept covers (the verifier `_covered` test) as new nodes, each with one mention whose quote is a sentence of the paper containing it (an exact substring, rule 3). No new edges (relations need model calls). k in {5, 10, 20, 30, all}; plus `NPC-all-nofilter` (every candidate, no coverage filter) as the recall ceiling of noun phrases as concepts.
- **Reported:** concept P / R / F1 and strict relation F1 (`IDXR1-P1`; relation F1 can move only through placement, since a longer new form can take a span from an existing node), against v5. The CR-057 rule is shown for reference only (concept recall +0.01, relation F1 -0.005); it cannot adopt anything here.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-060 Part B2 result: noun phrases as concepts do not improve recall usefully (diagnostic, not adopted)
- **Replay on v5 `cr055ballp`, 20 first-set papers, `IDXR1-P1`, $0** (`reports/cr060/part_b2_np_concepts.md`). Concept P / R / F1 (relation F1): control 0.356 / 0.751 / 0.483 (0.153); top 5 uncovered per unit +155 nodes: 0.262 / 0.756 / 0.389 (0.151); 10: +322, R 0.760, P 0.217; 20: +641, R 0.764, P 0.169; 30: +977, R 0.769, P 0.146; every uncovered candidate: +11,834 nodes, R 0.782 (+0.031), P 0.077, F1 0.140; every candidate without the coverage filter: R **0.406**, P 0.038, relation F1 0.025 (longer noun-phrase forms take the spans of existing nodes under longest match, so placement breaks).
- **Reading:** recall rises by at most +0.031 even when all 11,834 uncovered noun phrases become concepts (about 15 per gold mention gained); at the sizes the relation stage could afford (5-10 per unit) the gain is +0.004 / +0.008 while concept precision falls by 0.09 / 0.14. The missed gold mentions are mostly not uncovered noun phrases (see Part B sizing: 45 not chunks, 37 covered by a near-span item), and noun-chunk boundaries disagree with gold's more often than the generator's do. Not adopted; rule 24 unchanged; nothing in production changed.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-061: diagnostics page shows profile v10; the adopted A-all has a defect (finding for the owner)
- **Page:** `cumap.cr058.page` now applies the CR-060 span-identity step in memory (profile v10, variant `--span-identity`, default the adopted A-all); `--profile v5` rebuilds the CR-058 page as merged (`pipeline_v5.html`, `summary.*`, unchanged but for a `profile` field). v10 gates: pooled concept and relation counts equal the CR-060 replay (`reports/cr060/part_a.json`, new `part_a_rest.json`); all pass; source checkpoints unchanged.

R-acr is defective under r1key matching; R-head is not.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-062 pre-registration: concept F2 headline and a domain-free, more accurate verifier — written before any computation
- **Constraint (owner, 2026-10-30):** the pipeline must traverse domains: no component is trained or tuned on domain-specific (gold) information; gold only evaluates on train / dev. A learned verifier score (context embedding) is excluded.
- **(a) Metric:** concept **F2** (beta = 2, fixed now) becomes the headline concept number in CR-043 scorecards and the diagnostics page; F1 stays reported beside it. Adoption rules are unchanged (recall-first: concept recall >= +0.01, strict relation F1 >= -0.005).
- **(b) Verifier recall rules, sized by replay at $0** (approximate: the corrective-round interplay is not replayed) on the 57 train / dev papers (v11 runs): items the verifier dropped on F2 / F3 are rescued when
  - **V1 re-anchor:** the item's name occurs verbatim (case-insensitive) in the paper; the new quote is the sentence containing the occurrence nearest the original quote (an exact substring, rule 3); variant `para` (same paragraph as the original quote only) and `paper` (anywhere);
  - **V2 F3 tolerance:** the name's R1 key equals the R1 key of a word window inside the quote (hyphen / space / plural variants);
  - **V3:** a hard drop remains only when neither applies (the name is not in the paper).
  Reported: rescued items gold / non-gold, and concept P / R / F1 / F2 with the rescued items added as concepts (IDXR1-P1).
- **(c) Domain-free flag rules, sized at $0** on the final concepts of the same runs (counts of gold and non-gold nodes flagged, and gold mentions at risk): **V6 sentence role** (every evidence quote of the node is a citation sentence, an "e.g. / such as / for example" list or a parenthetical citation context); **V7 grammatical role** (in every sentence of the paper that contains the item, its head token is only a modifier: dependency `compound` / `amod` / `nmod` / `poss` / `appos`, never a subject, object or prepositional object). spaCy `en_core_web_sm` only, no training.
- **(d)** A paid arm (first 20 papers, 2 first) only if (b) adds concept recall >= 0.005 or (c) flags at least 3 non-gold nodes per gold node; otherwise CR-062 ends at $0. Cap $1.5.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-062 results ($0): concept F2 headline in place; recall rules V1-paper + V2 meet the trigger, flag rules do not
- **(a) Done:** CR-043 scorecards report concept P / R / **F2** (F1 beside, F2 computed from the counts for older scorecards); the diagnostics page shows concept F2 beside F1 in the stage tables and per-paper table. Adoption rules unchanged.
- **(b) Recall rules** (`cumap.cr062.measure`, `reports/cr062/sizing.md`, 57 papers, v11, approximate replay): control concept P / R / F2 0.349 / 0.747 / 0.609. V2 (F3 tolerance) rescues 5 gold / 24 non-gold items, recall +0.0012; V1-para + V2 10 / 34, +0.0026; **V1-paper + V2 17 / 63, recall +0.0060, F2 +0.0024**, precision -0.001. Items already in the final graph by another route are not counted.
- **(c) Flag rules:** V6 sentence role flags 171 non-gold / 208 gold nodes (0.82 per gold: citation and example contexts carry many gold datasets and models); V7 grammatical role 226 / 124 (1.82); either 376 / 307. Below the 3 : 1 trigger: not pursued.
- **Trigger:** met by (b) (+0.0060 >= +0.005). The pre-registered paid arm would test V1-paper + V2 on the first 20 papers; its expected concept-recall gain (about +0.006 pooled) is below the +0.01 adoption rule, so the arm is held for the owner before any spend.

## 2026-10-30 · CR-062 closed (owner): no paid arm
- Concept F2 headline adopted (reporting). Verifier re-anchoring (V1-paper + V2) recorded as small and positive (+0.006 recall at $0), not adopted, no arm run; flag rules V6 / V7 not pursued. Spend $0. Next: CR-063 (matcher fix for bracketed acronyms and trailing symbols).
